Preface, 


Y a novena is meant a devotion 
of nine days in honor of some 
mystery of our redemption to ob- 
tain a particular request from Our 
Lord through the merits of His sacred 
life and passion, or in honor of the 
Blessed Virgin or any of the saints, 
that by their intercession they may 
obtain for us favors from Almighty 
God. Novenas have always been 
considered a favorite mode of prayer. 
We know that God grants our peti- 
tions when we persevere in prayer. 
Often we pray, but we fail to obtain 
our object because we do not per- 
severe in our pleadings. God wishes 
to be importuned, and then when in 
His inscrutable providence He finds 
it for our good and for His own 
glory He grants our requests. 
Novenas may be made with any 
prayer. Those in this book are 
given as subjects and suggestions of 
what we should embody in our pe- 
1 


ii ; PREFACE. 


titions, that by borrowing the words 
of the prayer-book, we may offer to 
Almighty God holy sentiments which 
otherwise might be forgotten. Many 
other forms of prayers might be used: 
the Rosary said every day with the 
intention of gaining our requests, or 
confession and communion offered 
for the same purpose. We shoulé 
per‘orm a novena with the necessary 
condition of all prayers—that is, a 
great faith that God will grant us our 
requests, for Our Lord says: ‘‘ Ask, 
and you shall receive,’’ and perfect 
resignation to His holy will, though 
in His infinite wisdom He may with- 
hold the particular favor which we 
request. 

A beautiful example of persever- 
ance in prayer is given by Our Lord 
in Luke xi. 5-8: A neighbor im- 
portunes his friend in the depths of 
the night to get up and give hima 
loaf of bread, because a friend had 
arrived from a journey, and though 
he did not give it to him because he 
was a friend, but on account of his 
persevering prayer, he gave him all 
he wished A nine days’ devotion is 


PREFACE. ili 


this importunity in which we ask 
God to grant us the needful things, 
God gives, not because it is good for 
us, but because we have asked, and 
then in His providence He arranges 
that this gilt will not be a detriment 
to us. Besides the novenas there 
are devotions of greater and of lesser 
length. Such of these as have been 
establishe by pious custom and long 
usage are aiso given in this book,

PRAYER TO THE HOLY NAME. 


O MERCIFUL Jesus, Who didst in 

Thy early infancy commence 
Thy office of Saviour by shed- 
ding Thy precious blood and assum: 
ing for us that name which is above 
all names, we thank Thee for such 
early proofs of Thy infinite love. 
We venerate Thy sacred name in 
union with the profound respect of 
the angel who first announced it to 
the earth, and unite our affection to 
the sentiments of tender devotion 
which the adorable name of Jesus 
nas in all ages enkindlea in the 

17 


18 NOVENA IN HONOR OF THE 


hearts of Thy saints. Animated with 
a firm faith in Thy unerring word, 
and penetrated with confidence ia 
Thy mercy, we now most humbly 
remind Thee of the promise Thou 
hast made, that where two or three 
should assemble in Thy name Thou 
Thyself wouldst be in the mids: of 
them. Come, then, into the midst 
of us, most amiable Jesus, for it is 
in Thy sacred name we are assem- 
bled. Come into our hearts, that 
Thy Holy Spirit may pray in us and 
by us; and mercifully grant us, 
through that adorable name which 
is the joy of heaven, the terror of 
hell, the consolation of the afflicted, 
and the solid ground of our unlimited 
confidence, all the petitions we make 
in this novena. 

Oblessed Mother of our Redeemer, 
who didst participate so sensibly in 
the sufferings of thy dear Son when 
He shed His sacred ‘blood and as- 
sumed for us the name of fesus, 9d- 
tain for us, through that adorable 
name, the favors we petition in this 
novena. Beg also that the most 
ardent love may imprint in our hearts 


HOLY NAME OF JESUS. {9 


that sacred name ; that it may be al- 
ways in our minds and frequently on 
our lips; that it may be our defence 
in temptation, our refuge in danger 
during our lives, and our consolation 
and support in the hour of death. 
Amen,

I HAVE. BUT 

One desire—Jesus. 

One fear—to lose Jesus. 

One to rest on—Jesus. 

One home—the wounds of Jesus. 

One occupation—to converse with 
Jesus. 

Onz charge—to do the will of 
Jesus. 

One joy—to possess Jesus. 

One hope—to see Jesus. 

One grief—compassion for Jesus. 

One witness—the eye of Jesus. 

One consolation—to suffer . for 
Jesus. 

One glory—teo be despised for 
Jesus. 

One rest—to labor for Jesus. 

One refuge—the Heart of Jesus.

PRAYER TO:THE HOLY NAME OF JESUS. 


O coop Jesus! O most sweet 

Jesus! O Jesus, Son of the 
Virgin Mary, full of mercy and ten- 
derness ! O sweet Jesus, according 
to Thy great mercy have mercy on 
me! O most merciful Jesus, I im 
plore thee by that precious blood 
which Thou didst deign to shed for 
sinners, to wash away my iniquities 
and look upon me in my misery and 
unworthiness, humbly begging par- 
don, and calling upon this holy 
name, Jesus! O name of Jesus, 
name of sweetness! Name of Jesus, 
name of delights. Name of Jesus, 
name of comfort. For what is Jesus 
but Saviour? Therefore, Jesus, on 
account of Thy holy name, be to me 
a Jesus, and save me. Suffer me not 
to perish, whom Thou hast created 
out of nothing. O good Jesus! let 
not my iniquity overwhelm me, whom 
Thy omnipotent bounty hast made. 
O sweet Jesus! acknowledge what is 
Thine and efface what is not Thine. 
O most gentle Jesus! have mercy on 
me, while yet the time for mercy lasts, 


BIRTH OF OUR LORD JESUS CHRIST. 2: 


lest Thou condemn me in the day ot 
Thy judgment. The dead will not 
praise Thee, Lord Jesus, nor they 
who descend into hell. O most lov- 
ing Jesus! O most amiable Jesus! 
O meekest Jesus! O Jesus, Jesus, 
_ Jesus! take me into the number of 
Thine elect. O Jesus, salvation of 
those who believe in Thee! comfort 
of those who fly to Thee! O Jesus, 
Son of the Virgin Mary! infuse into 
me grace, wisdom, charity, chastity, 
and humility, that I may love Thee 
perfectly, praise Thee, enjoy Thee, 
serve Thee, and be glorified in Thee, 
with all those who call upon Thy 
name, Jesus. Amen.

of Our Lord Jesus Christ. 
{Feast, Dec. 25th. Novena begins Dec. 
16th and ends Christmas Eve.) 
PRAYER. 


Now begin, O my God, a novena 

in honor of the Incarnation of 
Thy divine Son. During these days 
I wish to be recollected and to with- 


26 NOVENA FOR THE FEAST OF THE 


draw myself as much as my avocations 
permit from worldly distractions, 
and to adore in silence the mysteries 
of the Word made flesh, and I ear- 
nestly desire that He be born in my 
heart. Come, O Lord Jesus; come, 
O Holy Spirit of truth and love, Who 
didst form the divine Infant in the 
bosom of the most pure Virgin. 

O Jesus, I wait for Thy coming as 
did the prophets and patriarchs ot 
old; with feeling I cry out with 
them : ‘‘Drop down dew, ye heavens, 
from above, and let the clouds rain 
down the just; let the earth open 
and give us the Redeemer ! ’’ 

O King, in comparison with Whom 
the potentates of this world are but 
a shadow, ‘let Thy kingdom 
come.’’ When will come upon us 
from on high that kingdom of justice, 
peace, and truth ? 

O most holy Virgin and blessed 
St. Joseph! obtain for us the grace 
to perform this novena with such 
attention, devotion, and ardent 
charity, as will entitle us to join 
the angels in rendering glory to 
God.

Let us say twelve Hail Marys in re- 
membrance of the care and solicitude 
shown by our blessed Lady towards the 
Infant Jesus till His twelfth year. Hail 
Mary, etc. ; 

Let us say three Our Fathers in honor 
of the years of Our Lord’s infancy. 
Our Father, etc. 

Let us make three aspirations to ins 
cline the Infant Jesus to turn His favor- 
able attention on us. 


O divine Infant of Bethlehem! 
Whom we adore and acknowledge to 
be our sovereign Lord, come and 
take birth in our hearts. Amen. 

O Infant Jesus! grant that each 
moment of our lives we may pay 
homage to that moment in which 
Thou didst begin the work of our 
salvation. Amen. 

O holy Mother of our Infant Sav- 
iour! obtain that we may so prepare 
ourselves for His coming as not to. 
be separated from Him for all eter- 
nity. Amen.

Mm”: holy Infant Jesus, true God 


and true man, our Saviour and

28 NOVENA FOR THE 


charity, humility, and bounty which 
Thou didst display in Thy infancy, 
graciously undertaken for the love of 
us, that Thou vouchsafe to grant us 
the favor we now beg, if it be for the 
honor of God and our salvation. 
[Here each one will ask in spirit the 
particular favor desired. | 

O most amiable Infant Jesus! we 
are most unworthy to be heard in this 
our petition ; but Thy holy Mother, 
the Virgin Mary, and the great St. 
Joseph, Thy foster-father while on 
earth, are worthy to be heard solicit- 
ing in our behalf. Then, O divine 
Infant! being mindful of their most 
sublime merits, especially those they 
acquired during the time they served 
Theein Thy infancy in Bethlehem, 
Thy flight into Egypt, and Thy child- 
nood at Nazareth, vouchsafe to grant 
our requests, and give us grace to 
promote the honor of Thy omnipo- 
tent infancy, to serve Thee with fidel- 
ity as domestic servants all the days 
of our lives, and to obtain a happy 
death, assisted in that last hour by 
the Blessed Virgin and St. Joseph, 
whose zeal for Thy honor will lead us 


FEAST OF CHRISTMAS. 29 


to praise and bless Thy divine mer- 
cies forever and ever. Amen. 


ASPIRATION. 


1 and blessed be the hour and 
moment in which the Son of 
God was born of the most pure Virgin 
Mary at midnight in Bethlehem in 
the piercing cold. In that hour 
vouchsafe, O my God! to hear my 
prayer and grant my desires, through 
the merits of Our Saviour Jesus 
Christ, and of His blessed Mother. 
Amen. 

It is piously believed that whoever 
recites the above prayer fifteen times a 
day, from the feast of St. Andrew 
(Nov. 30) until Christmas, will obtain 
what is asked.

Let us Pray. 


O BLESSED parents of the Infant 

Jesus, Mary and Joseph, if we 
came to solicit you to obtain for us 
worldly prosperity, honors, and com- 
forts, our petitions could never 
reach a God whose cradle is a man- 
ger ; a God, poor, weak, suffering, 
and humbled even to annihilation. 
But these are not our desires or peti- 
tions. No; we humbly and earnestly 
implore you to beg for us, at the 
_ crib of your infant Son, that, like 
Him, and as His true and faithful 
followers, we may live in true humil- 

3 


32 NOVENA FOR THE 


ity, disengagement from creatures, 
contempt of the world and its vani- 
ties, constant recollection, fervor,and 
fidelity in all our duties, and in the 
practice of the tenderest charity to 
our fellow-creatures. May we ever 
rejoice in our calling to the true faith, 
which binds us to be the followers 
and imitators of our humble Saviour. 
May we ever look with compassion- 
ate eyes on those who live but for 
this world, and continually pray that 
all may come to bless, to praise, and 
to love the Saviour Who comes to re- 
deem them. Amen.

duce Thy incarnate divinity and

To all who prepare themselves for 
he feast of Christmas by a novena is 
granted an indulgerce of 300 days, 
every day, and a plenary indulgence 
on any day during the novena, on 
Christmas, or any day of its octave, 
on the usual conditions. — Pius Vil 
Aug, 12, 1815; Piur VIII. july 9 1830

OFFERING TO THE ETERNAL FATHER. 


First Offering. —Eternal Father, I 
offer to Thy honor and glory, and 
for my own salvation and for the 
salvation of the whole world, the 
mystery of the birth of our divine 
Saviour. 

Glory be to the Father, etc. 

Second Offering.—Eternal Father, 
I offer to Thy honor and glory, and 
for my eternal salvation, the suffer- 
ings cf the most holy Virgin and 
St. Joseph in that long and weary 
journey from Nazareth to Bethle- 
hem. + offer Thee the sorrows of 
their hearts when they found no place 
wherein to shelter themselves when 
the Saviour of the world was to be 
born. 

Glory be to the Father, etc. 

Third Offering. —Eternal Father, I 
offer to Thy honor and glory, and for 
my salvation, the sufferings of Jesus 


HOLY CHILD JESUS. 37 


in the stable where He was born, 
the cold He suffered, the swad- 
dling-clothes which bound Him, 
the tears He shed, and His tender 
infant « ies. 

Glory be to the Father, etc. 

fourth Offering.—Eternal Father, 
I offer to Thy honor and glory, 
and for my eternal salvation, the 
pain which the holy Child Jesus 
felt in His tender body when He 
submitted to circumcision. I offer 
Thee His precious blood which 
then, for the first time, He shed 
for the salvation of the whole human 
race. 

Glory be to the Father, etc. 

Lifth Offering.—Eternal Father, I 
offer to Thy honor and glory, and 
for my eternal salvation, the humil- 
ity, mortification, patience, charity,’ 
and all the virtues of the Child 
Jesus; I thank Thee, and I love 
Thee, and I bless Thee without end 
for the ineffable mystery of the in- 
‘carnation of the Divine Word. 

Glory be to the Father, etc.

faithful who, with at least contrite 
heart and devotion, in public or in 
private, on any of the nine days preced-

the versicles and prayer.

46 NOVENA TO THE 


fAovena to the ‘fnfant Jesus of 
Prague. 


First Day. 


Ta us venerate the holy head of 
the divine Infant, in order to 
cbtain the three divine virtues of 
faith, hope, and charity. 


Aspiration. 


HAT do I see? What a bright 

glow falls from the beautiful 

crown that encircles Thy head! 

What veneration I feel in my heart, 

struck by the loveliness and majesty 

that shine from Thy dear and pre- 
cious crown ! 

Kings. only wear crowns. ‘Thou, 
too, art a King, dearest Jesus, the 
greatest and mightiest of all. Thou 
art a King from all eternity ! 

I recognize and acknowledge Thee 
as my King, as my highest and best — 
Lord; therefore I cast myself down 
at Thy feet and adore humbly Thy 
most holy head. 


UNFANT JESUS OF PRAGUE. 47 


Prayer. 


I EARNESTLY beseech Thee that 
Thou wouldst increase in me 
the light of faith. Make me firmly 
believe that Thy holy image repre- 
sents Thee as the eternally crowned 
King of heaven and earth, the Son 
of the heavenly Father, and of the 
Blessed Virgin Mary, who during 
Thy holy infancy nursed Thee so. 
carefully and served Thee so zeal- 
ously. Gracious and beneficent Je- 
sus, give me a well-grounded hope, 
since my sins fill me with fear. I 
know that little children do not 
cherish anger and are easily per- 
suaded. By the mystery of ‘Thy 
infancy, pardon me, I beseech Thee. 
Grant that I may love Thee in: all. 
and above all. May nothing extin- 
guish this flame of love! Grant me 
the grace to fulfil all the duties of my 
state faithfully, readily, and cheer- 
fully, and to bear patiently all the 
trouble and_ bitterness of life. 
Amen.

48 NOVENA TO THE 


Seconp Day. 


A eS us venerate the beautiful face 
of the divine Child, in order 
to receive the entire forgiveness of 
all our sins. 


Aspiration. 


O most adorable face of Jesus, I 
prostrate myself before thee 
and venerate and contemplate with 
admiration thy exquisite, beautiful 
form. 

Thou lovest to alter this Thy holy 
countenance according to the state 
of the soul that beholds Thee. Some- 
times Thou seemest quite angry and 
stern, and sometimes kind and loving. 
Sinners see Thy face covered with a 
dark veil, so that they cannot behold 
Thee until they have faithfully con~ 
fessed all their sins. Dost Thou 
wish by this alteration to point out 
our fickleness and to strengthen us it 
good ? 

O dearest Infant Jesus, let some 
rays from Thy countenance pierce 
my dark soul, that, enlightened 
thereby, I may see truly all the 


INFANT JESUS OF PRAGUE. 49 


misery of sin, wholly repent, sin- 
cerely confess, and faithfully atone 
for all my transgressions, that I may 
behold in peace Thy holy face. 


Prayer. 


EAR Infant Jesus, consider that 

Thy most sweet image re- 
mained seven long years in a dark 
room, dusty and forgotten, and ney- 
ertheless retained its ancient beauty. 
Cleanse the chamber of my soul 
from the dust and filth of sin, that 
it may once more be clad in the 
beauty it received in holy Baptism. 
Amen.

INFANT JESUS OF PRAGUE. 55 


heart of the dear Infant! Day and

Why is my heart so cold in its love,

ish motives that the heroic love of 
God and of my neighbor can find no 
place therein? What a miserable 
child of earth Iam! 

' So near the hearth-fire of eternal 
love, and yet so icy cold! What 
a purgatory I will have to suffer! 
And the chief cause of this calculat. 
ing wisdom of the world is pride, 
self-love, ambition—in a word, ygo- 
tism.

Prayer. 


GREAT and most wonderful God, 
represented in the form of a 
little infant, how deep down in my 
heart do I feel my shame! ‘Thou art 
eternal majesty and perfect sanctity, 
and ‘Thou art humble of heart ; I, 
who am wretchedness itself, and full 
of the misery of sin, am filled with 
pride and self-esteem, and have 
therefore so little peace of heart. 
Truly, because I am so poor in 
virtue, Thou hast every reason to 
turn away from me Thy most beauti- 
ful countenance. In spite of this, 
however, I am always ready to exalt 
myself above others, and to blame 
and criticise them. I know my mis- 
ery, and yet I would fain be praised 
and rewarded, and preferred to 
others. The least humiliation or any 
neglect on the part of my fellow- 
creatures pains me, whilst Thou art 
so full of meekness and of long- 
suffering beyond measure, and hast 
so much patience with the children 
of men. 
Divine Heart of my dearest Jesus,

58 NOVENA TO THE 


This miraculous image lay pros 
trate in the dust, mutilated and for. 
gotten, for seven long years. Now 
it stands on many altars throughout 
the world, and is there venerated, 
while thousands of likenesses of it are 
spread everywhere and are honored 
in convents and families. First trod- 
den under foot and neglected, then 
venerated andshowered with gifts by 
kings and princes, cardinals, bishops, 
and priests, regular and secular—all 
conditions of. men. Is not this 
miraculous? What man could have 
brought this about? For all this I 
praise Thee, and I am greatly rejoiced 
to learn of and believe in Thy exaita- 
tion. As happened to Thy favored 
image, so also was Thy divine-human 
person treated. Thou wast an out- 
cast among men, mocked and de- 
spised, scourged and crowned with 
thorns, cast into prison and a mur- 
derer preferred before Thee, made 
the jest of the people, and aow Thou 
art glorious in heaven and on earth. 
At Thy name every knee must bend, 
and Thou wilt come to judge the 
world. What a wonderful change ! 


INFANT JESUS OF PRAGUE. 59 


How can a man who thinks of all this 
in his heart remain cold, unbelieving, 
and indifferent ? 


Prayer. 


O MOST gracious Child Jesus! I 
come now to Thee to beg from 
Thee most earnestly the grace of a 
happy death. When my last hour 
arrives, do Thou come to me in the 
holy Viaticum and remain with me, 
and bring unto me Thy Virgin 
Mother and St. Joseph. Soothe my 
agony, diminish my anxiety, give 
me courage to meet all assaults, and 
grant me the grace to surrender 
willingly my earthly life in satisfac- 
tion for my sins, in order that I may 
so gain the indulgence for the dying, 
the last grace of holy mother Church. 
Bless me, therefore, with Thy little 
hand when the priest or some other 
merciful Samaritan blesses me. 

On earth Thou didst live and suffer 
twelve thousand one hundred and 
aynety days; that makes two hundred 
and ninety-two thousand five hundred 
and sixty hours ; and then Thou didst

die poor and neglected and despised 
and mocked by the world. 

By Thy agony in the Garden of

blood Thou didst shed, I beseech 
Thee grant me a happy death. If 
the end is good, all will be well. St. 
Joseph and St. Barbara, pray that I 
may receive this great grace. Amen.

PRAYER OF SAINT MARGARET MARY 


Sie Father, permit me to 
offer Thee the heart of Thy 
well-beloved Son, Jesus Christ, as 


#£RAST OF THE SACRED HEART. 67 


fe ofters Himself to Thee in sacri- 
fice. Receive this oblation for me, 
together with all the desires, sen- 
timents, affections, movements, and 
acts of this sacred heart. They are 
all mine, since He immolates Him- 
self for me, and henceforth I wish to 
have no desires byt His. Receive 
them in satisfaction for my sins, and 
in thanksgiving for all Thy benefits. 
Receive them, that through His 
merits Thou mayst grant me all the 
graces necessary for me, especially 
the grace of final perseverance. Re- 
ceive them as so many acts of love, 
adoration, and praise, which I offer 
Thy divine majesty, since it is by the 
heart of Jesus Thou art worthily 
honored and glorified. Amen. 

For making the novena an indulgence 
of 300 days every day and a plenary in- 
dulgence on the day immediately after 
the novena or in its octave.—Pius IX., 
Nov. 26, 1876.

thee I find consolation when

AN OFFERING. 


SACRED Heart of our dear Re- 

deemer! we adore thee with 
all the powers of our souls ; we con- 
consecrate them forever to thee, 
‘with each of our thoughts, words, 
actions, and our whole being. We 
‘offer to thee, O divine Heart! all 
those acts of adoration, love, and 
glory which, whilst in this mortal 
life, thou didst render to thy eter- 
nal-Father. Be thou the repairer 
of our deficiencies, the protector of 
our lives, and our refuge and security 
at the hour of death. Grant us, 
through the merits of that anguish 
and bitterness which for us thou 
didst suffer through the whole course 
of thy mortal life, a perfect con- 
trition for our sins; grant us a con. 
stant disgust of all worldly allure- 
ments, an ardent desire of eternal 


SACRED HEART OF JESUS. 7i 


glory, and a lively hope of partaking 
of thy infinite merits. 

O most loving Heart of Jesus ! we 
present to thee these our humble 
supplications, not for ourselves only, 
but for all those whom we earnestly 
recommend to thee in this novena 
JN.N.]. It is our ardent wish, O 
our dearest Lord! that all may join 
us in spirit to serve and obey Thee. 
Accept these our humble petitions, 
and graciously grant our requests, 
through Thy infinite mercy. \mpress 
us, O Lord! with due sentiments of 
gratitude for the beneficent tender- 
ness of Thy divine heart towards us, 
forlorn sinners ; receive us in the yet 
gaping wound of that adorable heart, 
that in it we may admire Thy divine 
attributes, practise Thy heavenly 
virtues, find the effect of Thy sacred 
passion, and our poor afflicted souls 
be thereby encouraged to shelter 
themselves within Thy sacred wounds,

cold hearts ! thy delight is to remain

us that cruel flagellation at the pillar

through love of us embraced the 
heavy cross and bore its weight to

Father, that most inflamed and hum 
ble heart of Jesus which through 
iove of us laid itself on the hard 
wood of the cross and offered its 
sacred hands and feet to cruel exe- 
cutioners to be transpierced with iron 
nails. Behold, O heavenly Father, 
this sacrifice of love offered to Thee 
for us poor sinners in the heart of 
Thy dear Son; it is wounded, it 
bleeds; it expires through the in- 
finite love it has for us. My God, 
if we have offended Thee, will not 
the sight of this heart suffice to ap- 
pease Thy anger? Receive it, O 
merciful Father, in atonement for 
our many offences: it is most worthy 
of Thy love and everlasting com. 
placency. Amen.

FORM OF CONSECRATION. 


Issued on Oct. 17, 1925, by His Holiness 
Pope Pius XI, for the annual consecration of 
the:-human race to the Sacred Heart on the feast 
of Christ the King, the last Sunday of October. 


(b" SWEET JFsus, Redeemer of 
the human race, look down 
upon us, humbly prostrate before 
Thy. altar. .We are. Thine and 
Thine we wish to be; but to be 
more surely united with Thee, be- 
hold each one of us freely. conse- 
crates himself to-day to Thy Most 
Sacred Heart. Many indeed have 
never known Thee; many, too, de- 
spising Thy precepts, have rejected 
Thee. Have mercy on them all,

most merciful Jesus, and draw 


them to Thy Sacred Heart. Be 
Thou King, O Lord, not only of 
the faithful who have never for- 
saken Thee, but also of the prodi- 
gal children who have abandoned 
« Thee; grant that they may quickly 
return to their Father’s house, lest 
they die of wretchedness and hun- 
ger. Be Thou King of all those 
who are still involved in the dark- 
ness of idolatry or of Islamism, and 
refuse not to draw them all into 
the light and kingdom of God. 
Turn Thine eyes of mercy toward 
the children of that race, once Thy 
chosen people. Of old they called 
down upon themselves the blood of 
the Saviour; may it now descend 
upon them a laver of redemption 
and of life. Grant, O Lord, to 
Thy Church assurance of freedom 
and immunity from harm; give 
peace and order to all nations, and 
make the earth resound from pole 
to pole with one cry: Praise to the 
divine Heart that wrought our 
salvation; to it be glory and honor 
forever, Amen.

ASPIRATION TO THE SACRED HEART. 


My God, Thou seest the 

wounds of my soul; I disclose 
them to Thee: have mercy on me? 
{ am sick, and Thou art a charitable 
Physician ; I am full of misery, and 
Thou art Mercy itself. All my hope 
is in the infinite greatness of Thy 
bounty.

PRAYER OF THE VENERABLE FATHER 
DE LA COLOMBIERE TO THE 
SACRED HEART, 


oO SacRED Heart of Jesus! do 

Thou teach me an entire for- 
getfulness of myself, since there is 
no other way of entering into Thee. 
Grant that I may do nothing that is 
not worthy of Thee. ‘Teach me 
what I ought to do to attain to Thy 
pure love, with the desire of which 
Thou hast inspired me. I feel in 
myself a great wish to please Thee, 
and a great inability to do so with- 
out a special light and assistance 
which I can look for only from Thee. 
Do thou accomplish Thy will, O 
Lord, in me. I oppose it, I well 
know; but I would fain not do so. 
It is for Thee, O divine Heart s/ 
Jesus, to do all. Thine alone shall 
be the glory if I become a saint, and 
it is for this alone that I desire to he 
perfect. _ Amen.

BLESSING OF THE SACRED HEART, 


AY the grace and blessing of the 

Sacred Heart be with you; the 
peace of the Sacred Heart encom- 
pass you; the merits of the Sacred 
Heart plead for you; the love of the 
Sacred Heart inflame you; the sor- 
rows of the Sacred Heart console 
you; the zeal of the Sacred Heart 
animate you; the virtues of the 
Sacred Heart shine forth in every 
word and work, and may the joys of 
the beatific vision be your eternal 
recompense. Amen.

bear the lesson, teach me thy blessed 
ways. Amen. 


A MOST EFFICACIOUS, PRAYER TO THE 
SACRED HEART.

that there is but one thing 
impossible to thee: to be without 
pity for those who are in suffering or 
distress. Look down, O Jesus, we 
beseech Thee, on us, and grant us the 
grace for which we humbly implore 
of Thee through the immaculate 
Heart of Thy most sorrowful Mother, 

‘to whom Thou didst confide us as 
her children, and whose prayers are 
all-powerful with Thee. Amen.

This Rosary is a perfect treasury oi 
induigences, for the prayers which 
tompose it are rich in this respect. 

There is no need for special beads; 
those of the Blessed Virgin are suffi- 
cient. We may say the Rosary of the 
Sacred Heart everywhere, for it is 
easy to learn, and may be recited ina 
few minutes.

ACTS OF ADORATION AND REPARATION 
TO JESUS IN THE BLESSED 
SACRAMENT. 


T; ROFOUNDLY I adore Thee, 
Jesus, hid beneath the 

mystic emblems ; I acknowledge Thee 
very God andvery Man. Accept this 
act of adoration, by which I fain 
would make Thee reparation for the 
cold hearts of so many of Thy peopie 
who pass before Thy holy temples, 
nay, before Thy very tabernacle, 
where, hour after hour, Thou dost 
deign to dwell with loving impatience 
to give Thyself to be Thy people’s 
food, and yet do not even bend the 


i090 06©—° |S NOVENA TO-JESUS™ 


knee before Thee, their God; Sut, 
like the Israelites in the wilderness, 
seem, by their indifference, to loathe 
this bread of heaven. I offer Thee, 
then, Thine own most precious blood 
which Thou didst shed from the 
wound in Thy left foot in reparation 
for this hateful coldness ; and, enter- 
ing in spirit within this same wound, I 
cry aloud, in never-ending praise: 
O sacrament most holy ! O sacra- 
ment divine ! 
All praise and all thanksgiving be 
every moment thine. 
Our Father, Hail Mary, Glory be 
to the Father.

Jesus ; I acknowledge Thy presence 
in this Most Holy Sacrament. By 
this act of adoration fain would I 
make amends for Thy people, sa 
many of whom seem as though they 
knew Thee not ; but, while they see 
Thee go to the poor sick, to be their 
strength in their great journey to 
eternity, leave Thee unescorted, and 
bardly give Thee even one outward 
sign of homage. I offer Thee, in 
separation of this coldness, Thine

ages yet to come.

the little strength I have; and fain 
would I adore Thee with all the per-

Pious Considerations. 


I. 


hn” often do we say to ourselves, 

‘«T should indeed have been 
happy had I lived at the time when 
Jesus was really upon the earth. Oh, 
to have sat at His feet on the moun- 
tain, or by the seaside, or away in 
the loneliness of the desert, where 
He went to pray! Not a thought of 
my heart but I should have told Him, 
not a sorrow but I should have laid 
before Him. How His adorable 
presence would have cheered and 
sanctified my soul! And surely He 
Who fed the hungry, and gave sight 
to the blind, and forgave the worst 
sins, and even raised the dead and 
gave them once more to the arms of 
the mothers that mourned them, 
surely He would have given me all I 
stood in need of, and how holy and 
how happy I should have been!”’ 
Dear child, what you would then 
haw done you can do this very huur. 


{N THE BLESSED SACRAMENT. 107 


Jesus is near you still. In the soli- 
tude of the lonely church, where the 
lamp burns softly and all is stili 
around, is the same Jesus Who was 
on the mountain, and by the seaside, 
and in the lone desert ; and He has 
- the self-same loving heart, and the 
self-same divine power to aid. You 
do not see Him, it is true, but neither 
did the blind people whom He cured, 
only they knew He was there, and 
they followed Him. What matter 
that He is hidden from our vicwr 
we know that He is there. 

‘““He hides Himself,’’ says the 
Curé d’Ars, ‘‘as in a prison, and 
says to us: ‘You do not see Me, 
but of what moment is thate Ask 
of Me what you wish, and J will 
grant it to you.’ ”’ 


II. 


C1) Vag Jesus was preaching im Ju- 
dea, do you think that it would 
have been easy to gain access to Him 
and speak with Himalone? Not sa; 
many of the crowd that followed Him 
beheld Him only from a distance. 


108 NOVE™ ‘XO JESUS 


and it was but a small number of 
privileged souls who had the happi- 
ness of private conversation with 
Him. 

Far hizppier are we, for in tne 
Eucharist Jesus waits to speak with 
each one of us in particular, that we 
may tell Him the wants of our in- 
most heart, and to obtain from Him 
all graces specially necessary for us. 


Ill. 


6” HERE are many souls, pious souls, 

that avoid sin most carefully ; 
they are anxious-minded, sometimes 
almost despairing ; or they are care- 
less, weak, weary, without zeal in the 
accomplishment of their duties, or 
zeal or charity for their neighbors ; 
and why? Because they neglect to 
visit Jesus in the Eucharist; they 
will not come to Him to tell Him 
their sorrows, to ask His aid, to 
listen to His divine counsel breathed 
into the core of their listening hearts, 
which wouid send them forth filled 
with energy, strength, zeal, and de- 
votion. Omy child! accustom your- 
self to go often to Jesus, to. see in 


IN THE BLESSED SACRAMENT. I0G 


Mim the Father Who forgives your 
faults, the Physician Who cures yout 
wounds, the Friend Who loves you 
faithfully. 

_ Do not think the half-hour lost 
that you spend with the King of 
heaven ; you will not leave Hiia with- 
out gifts and benefits ; your work will 
be better done, your character be 
more pliant, your temper sweeter. 
From no cther visit can you gain all 
this, and you have sometimes, feel- 
ing a desire to unburden your heart, 
sought that comfort in a visit to 
some dear and intimate friend; of 
all friends, Jesus alone can truly as- 
sist you. 

iv, 
AL visit to the Blessed Sacrament 

F should be composed of pious 
reading, conversation with Jesus, and 
prayers for grace. 

The ‘‘ Imitation of Christ ’’ is ad- 
mirably suited for visits to the Blessed 
Sacrament. it is easy to imagine in 
studying its beautiful chapters that 
each word is actually addressed to 
one’s self by the lips of Jesus in the 


{Io NOVENA TO JESUS 


tabernacle. Many persons commence. 
all their visits by reading slowly some 
of these chapters, pausing when the 
heart seems to speak to Jesus, and 
sometimes passing the whole half 
hour thus, reading, speaking, listen- 
ing to Jesus, and letting the sweet- 
ness of the hour sink into their hearts. 
St. Liguori’s book of ‘‘ Visits to the 
Blessed Sacrament’”’ gives for each 
day in the month a little lecture of 
this kind, each full of sweetness and 
devotion. It is impossible to study 
them without being moved and 
drawn to the love of God. 

Acts of Faith and Adoration, Hope 


and Charity, Contrition and Reparation 
should be made.

faa CHRIST complained to thp 
servant of God, Sister Margare 
Mary Alacoque, of the ingratitude ox 
men to Him in this Sacramenr of 
Love. To make her understand the 
love with which He dwells ou our 
altars, He showed her His heart 
on a throne of flames, surrounded 
with thorns and surmounted by 4 


IN THE BLESSED SACRAMENT. Iti 


cross, and said to her: ‘‘ Behold that 
heart, which has loved men so ten- 
derly, which has reserved nothing, 
and which has been even consumed ta 
show its love for them. But in re- 
turn the greater part of them treat 
Me with ingratitude by their irrever- 
ence and by their contempt of My 
love in this sacrament. And what is 
most painful to Me is that they are 
hearts which are consecrated to Me.’’ 
Christians do not visit Jesus Christ 
because they do not love Him. They 
spend entire hours in the society of 
friends; and they feel tediousness in 
conversing half an hour with Jesus 
Christ. Some will say: Why does 
not Jesus Christ give me His love? 
I answer: If you do not banish the 
world from your heart, how can di- 
vine love enter it? Ah! if you could 
say with sincerity what St. Philip 
Neri said at the sight of the Holy 
Sacrament—‘‘ Behold my love! be- 
hold my iove!’’—you would not 
feel tediousness it: spending hours 
and entire days before the Blessed 
Sacrament. 

To souls enainoured of God, hours 

R 


{12 NOVENA TO JESUS 


spent before Jesus in the Blessed Sac- 
rament appear moments. St. Fran- 
cis Xavier labored the whole day for 
the salvation of souls; and what was 
his repose at night? It consisted in 
remaining before the Holy Sacra- 
ment. St. John Francis Regis, that 
great missionary of France, after hav- 
ing spent the entire day in preaching 
and hearing confessions, went at 
night to the church, and, having 
sometimes found it shut, he remained 
outside the door, exposed to the cold 
and wind, to pay homage, at least at 
a distance, to his beloved Lord. St. 
Aloysius Gonzaga wished to remain 
always before the Holy Sacrament, 
out was forbidden to do so by his 
superiors. In passing by the altar, 
he felt himself drawn by Jesus to re- 
main, but was compelled by obe- 
- dience to depart. Hence he would 
lovingly say to his Saviour: ‘* With- 
draw from me, O Lord, withdraw § 
Lord, do not draw me; allow me to 
depart; obedience obliges me to go 
away.’’ If, my brother, you do not 
feel this love for Jesus, endeavor at 
least to visit Him very frequently. 


IN THE BLESSED SACRAMENT. 113 


rf. will inflame your heart. Do you 
feel cold? ‘‘Approach the fire,”’ 
says St. Catharine of Siena, Ah} 
happy you if Jesus, by His grace, 
inflames you with His love. Then 
you will certainly no longer love the 
goods of this world; on the contrary 
you will despise them.

through the love which Thou

all who come to visit Thee, I be-

Sacrament of the Altar. From the 
abyss of my nothingness I adore 
Thee, and I thank Thee jor all the 
favors which Thou hast bestowed 
upon me, particularly for having 
given me Thyself in this sacrament, 
for having given me for my advocate 
Thy most holy Mother, Mary, and

for having called me to visit Thee 
in this church. 

I this day salute Thy most loving 
heart, and I wish to salute it for 
three ends: first, in thanksgiving for 
this great gift; secondly, in com- 
pensation for all the injuries Thou 
hast received from Thy enemies in 
this sacrament; thirdly, I wish by 
this visit to adore Thee in all places 
in which Thou art least honored and 
most abandoned in the Holy Sacra- 
ment. My Jesus, I love ‘hee with 
my whole heart. I am sorry for hay- 
ing hitnerto offended Thy infinite 
goodness. I purpose, with the as- 
sistance of Thy grace, never more to 
offend Thee; and at this moment, 
miserable as I am, I consecrate my 
whole being to Thee. I give Thee 
my entire will, all my affections and 
desires, and all that I have. From 
this day forward do what Thou wilt 
with me and with whatever belongs 
tome. I ask and desire only Thy 
holy love, the gift of final persever 
ance, and the perfect accomplishment 
of Thy will. I recommend to The» 
the souls in purgatory, particular] 


FEAST OF THE PRECIOUS BLOOD. II§ 


those who were most devoted to the 
Blessed Sacrament and to most holy 
Mary; and I also recommend to 
Thee all poor sinners. Finally, my 
dear Saviour, I unite all my affec- 
tions with the affections of Thy most 
loving heart; and, thus united, I 
offer them to Thy eternal Father, and 
I entreat Him, in Thy name and for 
Thy sake, to accept them.

Wovena to the holy Face of Our 
Lord Jesus Christ, 

(This special devotion is assigned for 
the month of April.)* 
INSTRUCTION. 

VERY one knows that at the recita- 
tion of the stations there is one 
commemorating the pious act of St. 


I20 NOVENA TO THE HOLY FACE 


Veronica, who offered to Our Lord a 
handkerchief that He might wipe His - 
blood-stained countenance thereon. 
Our Lord, having used it, gave it back 
to her, leaving upon it, as a recom- 
pense, the impress of His sacred face. 
Tradition informs us that St. Veronica 
carried this sacred picture to Rome. 
At present it is kept in a magnificent 
shrine in St. Peter’s. Every year on 
Good Friday it is shown to the people 
and the blessing given with it. A de- 
scription of it is given by an ancient 
writer, and is as follows: “The head 
of Christ is everywhere transpierced 
with thorns. The forehead is bleeding, 
the eyes swollen and bloodshot, the 
face pale and livid. Upon the right 
cheek the cruel mark of the blow given 
by Malchus with his iron gauntlet 
sorrowfully attracts observation, as 
also the spittle of the Jews with which 
the sacred cheeks were stained. The 
nose is a little flattened and bleeding. 
the mouth open and filled with blood, 
the beard torn in several places, and 
the hair also on one side.” For a long 
time it was forbidden under pain of ex- 
communication to make copies of this 
picture, but Pius IX., who loved the 
devotion greatly, permitted prints to 
be made on linen or silk and distributed 
for the devotion of the people. These 


OF OUR LORD JESUS CHRIST. I21 


prints are touched to the real picture, 
and are, moreover, furnished with the 
seal of the keeper of the Vatican relics. 
so as to authenticate them and to au- 
thorize the use of them for devotional 
purposes. Confraternities have been 
established in various parts of the 
world, and new ones are constantly 
aggregated to the principal one in 
Tours, France. 

Indulgences have been granted by our 
Holy Father Leo XIII. to this devotion. 
It has been supposed by some that it 
has been forbidden, but this is not so. 
It is not a public devotion used in 
churches, like the devotions which are 
of long standing in the special ap- 
proved prayers for public worship; 
but this devotion may be used in pri- 
vate, and to encourage its use plenary 
induleences are granted on the day of 
admission to the Confraternity of the 
Holy Face and at the hour of death.

LESSED be God. 
Blessed be His holy name. 
Blessed be Jesus Christ, true God 
and true Man. 
Blessed be the name of Jesus. 
Blessed be His most sacred heart. 


422 NOVENA TO THE HOLY FACE 


Blessed be Jesus in the Most Holy 
Sacrament of the Altar. 

Blessed be the great Mother of God, 
Mary, most holy. 

Blessed be her holy and immaculate 
conception. 

Blessed be the name of Mary, Vir- 
gin and Mother. 

Blessed be St. Joseph, her most chaste 
spouse. 

Blessed be God in His angels and in 
His saints. 


Indulgence of two years for every public 
recital after Mass or Benediction.—Leo XIII, 
February 2, 1897.

THE GOLDEN ARROW. 


The prayer called ‘‘ The Golden Ar- 
row” was given by our blessed Lord te 
Sister Sainte-Pierre to be said as a rep. 
aration for blasphemies. 

@* the most holy, most sacred, 

most adorable, most mysterious, 
and unutterable name of God be 
praised, loved, adored, and glorified 
in heaven, on earth, and in hell by 
all God’s creatures, and by the Sacred 
Heart of Our Lord and Saviour Jesus 
Christ in the Most Holy Sacrament - 
of the Altar. Amen.

(For the Feast of Pentecost.) 
INSTRUCTION. 


AITH teaches us that we cannot of 
our own power do a Single action 
ofa supernatural value without the help 


NOVENA TO THE HOLY GHOST. 135 


of the Holy Ghost. We are rational 
beings, indeed, but we have lost the 
friendship of God, and though this 


intimacy has been re-established, we 


must have the grace and inspiration of 
the Holy Spirit before we can do any- 
thing which will be meritorious to 
eternal life. We should, therefore, 
have a devotion to the Holy Ghost and 
ask Him in our frequent prayers to 
enlighten our mind, to fill our heart 
with a great love of God, and to direct 
our steps in the path of innocence and 
purity. 

What a glorious being a Christian 
endowed with the seven gifts of the 
Holy Ghost is! how full is his soul of 
the intelligence of heavenly things! 
Samuel, the youthful prophet, was full 
of this spirit when he cried out: 
‘*Speak, Lord, Thy servant heareth.” 
He does not know much of the sciences 
that worldlings pride themselves in, but 
he knows how to love God and to make 
himself agreeable to all about him 
His worship comes from the heart. He 
henors God by faithfully doing His 
holy will. 


THE SEVEN CIFTS OF THE HOLY 
GHOST. 


1. Wisdom is the first gift, and in- 
fuses into our hearts the other gifts of 
the Holy Spirit; according to the words 
of the Sacred Scriptures, ‘‘ All good 
things have come to me by wisdom.” 


{36 NOVENA TO THE HOLY GHOST 


Solomon asked it of God in preference 
to all other gifts. Wisdom surpasses 
all riches,and whatsoever a man has a 
desire for in this world to give him 
pleasure is not to be compared to it, 
its ways are bountiful ; it is the tree of 
life to him who possesses it. 

2. Understanding is the second gift, 
and enlightens our mind in regard to 
the truths of our holy religion Faith 
grows by means of the intelligence, 
and becomes strong and well founded 
on reason; the beautiful truths and 
maxims of the Scriptures are well un- 
derstood, and the words become true, 
spoken in them, that God has hid His 
marvels from the wise of this world 
and revealed them to the little ones. 

3. Counsel guides our life. Wise 
men have always maintained the axiom 
not to do anything without taking 
sounsel. Joas of old was faithful to 
God and to the practice of virtue as 
long as he listened to the advice of 
Joiada. The counsels we receive from 
the Holy Ghost are the ones inculcated 
by the divine wisdom herself. 

4. Fortitude makes us strong in God. 
That same Spirit that inspires us with 
good resolutions gives us at the same 
time strength and courage to put them 
into execution. This virtue made the 
martyrs and confessors so courageous 
to profess the faith of Christ. This in- 
terior determination makes us triumph 
over all the obstacles that continually 


NOVENA Tu tHE HOLY GHCST. 137 


present themselves in the path of out 
salvation, and makes us snffer with 
resignation all the trials of life. 

5. Knowledge. The Holy Ghost 
teaches us all those things which are 
useful for us to know—the way of our 
salvation without which all human 
science is vain. What a difference is 
there between one that is only the 
disciple of men and one that is the 
disciple of that Holy Spirit that speaks 
to the heart. By means of this sacred 
knowledge the soul knows the best 
means to arrive at its last end, which 
is God. 

6. Piety makes us love God and 
gives us the grace to feel an attraction 
for everything that has reference to 
the service of God; by it we gladly 
study God, to know Him the better and 
to practise the precepts of His divine 
law and feel the truth of the words: 
‘*-One day in Thy temple, O Lord, is 
more delicious than thousands in the 
service of the world.” 

7, The fear of the Lord, the seventh 
gift and virtue of the Holy Ghost, is 
the beginning of wisdom. It inspires 
the mind with a deep respect mingled 
with a great love for God; it places 
before our mind on the one side the 
strict justice with which He judges oui 
lives, and on the other the mercy whick 
He shows for His children. 

Our Holy Father Leo XIII., unde’ 
date cf May 9, 1807, has directed tha 


138 NOVENA TO THE HOLY GHOST. 


in every parish throughout the world 
a special novena be celebrated every 
year in honor of the Holy Ghost, in 
preparation for the feast of Pentecost. 
The prayers to be recited are the 
Veni Creator (p. 145), with the Litany 
of the Saints (p. 546), or the Rosary 
of the Blessed Virgin. To all who de. 
voutly recite these prayers, whether in 
the church or at home, he grants an 
indulgence of seven years and seven 
quarantines for each day of the novena, 
and a plenary indulgence any one day 
of the novena, on the usual condi- 
tions. The same indulgences niay be 
gained any day of the week between 
Pentecost and Trinity Sunday. 


ACT OF OBLATION TO THE HOLY 
GHOST. 


Q* my knees before the great 

cloud of witnesses, I offer my- 
self, soul and body, to Thee, eternal 
Spirit of God. I adore the bright- 
ness of Thy purity, the unerring 
keenness of Thy justice, and the 
might of Thy love. Thou art the 
strength and the light of my soul. 
In Thee I live and moveand am. I 
desire never to grieve Thee by un- 
faithfulness to grace, and I pray with 
allmy heart to be kept from the 
smallest sin against Thee. Make me 


NOVENA TO THE HOLY GHOST. 139 


faithful in every thought, and grant 
that I may always listen to Thy 
voice, watch for Thy light, and _fol- 
low Thy gracious inspirations. I 
cling to Thee, and give myself to 
Thee, and ask Thee, by Thy com- 
passion, to watch over me in my 
weakness. Holding the pierced feet 
of Jesus, and looking at His five 
wounds, and trusting to His precious 
blood, and adoring His open side 
and stricken heart, I implore Thee, 
adorable Spirit, Helper of my in- 
firmity, so to keep m2 ti: Thy grace 
that I may never sin against Thee 
with the sin which Thou canst not 
forgive. Give me grace, O Holy 
Ghost, Spirit of the Father and the 
Son, to say to Thee, always and 
everywhere, ‘‘ Speak, Lord, for Thy 
servant heareth.’’ 

O Spirit of Wisdom, preside over 
all my thoughts, words, and actions 
from this hour till the moment of 
my death. 

Spirit of Understanding, enlighten 
and teach me. 

Spirit of Counsel, direct my inex- 
perience. 


‘140 NOVENA TO THE HOLY GHOST. 


Spirit of Knowledge, instruct my 
ignorance. 

Spirit of Piety, make me fervent in 
good works. 

Svirit of Fear, restrain me from 
evil. 

Spirit of Peace, give me Thy peace. 

Heavenly Spirit, make me _ per- 
severe in the service of God, and 
enable me to act on all occasions 
with goodness and benignity, pa- 
tience, charity, joy, longanimity, 
mildness, and fidelity. Let the 
heavenly virtues of modesty, con- 
tinency, and chastity adorn the 
temple Thou hast chosen for Thy 
abode. O Spirit of Holiness, by Thy 
all-powerful grace preserve my soul 
from the misfortune of sin. Amen.

ACT OF FAITH. 


LT most firmly believe, because God, 
Who is the infallible Truth, hath 
so revealed to the holy Catholic 
Church, and through the Church te 
us, that there is one only God in three 
divine persons, equal and distinct, 
Father, Son, and Holy Ghost ; that 
the Son became man by taking to 
Himself flesh and a human soul 
through the operation of the Holy 
Ghost in the womb of the most pure 
Virgin Mary; that He died for us 
upon the cross, rose again, ascended 
into heaven, and from thence shall 
come again, at the end of the world, 
to judge all the living and the dead, 
to give paradise forever to the good 
and hell to the wicked ; moreover, 
for the same motive I believe all 
that the sarme holy Church believes 
and teaches.

AN ACT OF FAITH IN THE IMMACU- 
LATE CONCEPTION. 


oLty Mary, Mother of God, I 

firmly believe in thy immac- 
ulate conception ; I bless God for 
having granted thee this glorious 
privilege ; I thank Him a thousand 
times for having taught it to me by 
the infallible voice of the Church. 
Receive. my heart, O immaculate 
Virgin! I give it up to thee without 
reserve; purify it, guard it, never 
give it back to me. Preserve it in 
thy love and in the love of Jesus 
during time and eternity.

Come, Holy Ghost, etc., as on

Mm holy Mary, chosen and

by the Most Holy Trinity to be the 
Mother of the only-begotten Son of 
the eternal Father, foretold by the 
prophets, longed for by the patri- 
archs, desired by all nations, sanc- 
tuary and living temple of the Holy 
Ghost, sun without blemish, because 
conceived free from original sin, 
mistress of heaven and of earth, queen 
of angels, humbly prostrate at thy

joicing that the year has brought 
round again the memory of thy sweet 
nativity. With all our hearts we 
pray thee to vouchsafe in thy good. 
ness to come down again and be 
born in spirit in our souls, that, led 
captive by thy loveliness and sweet

the nine months thou didst pass 
within the cloister of thy mother’s 
womb, and say devoutly : 

1. We hail thee, Mary, who, 
sprung from the royal line of David, 
didst come forth from the light of 
heaven with high honor from the 
womb of holy Anne, thy most happy 
mother.

2. We hail thee, heavenly babe, 
white dove of purity, who, despite 
the infernal serpent, wast conceived 
free from the taint of Adam’s sin. 

Hail Mary, etc. 

3. We hail thee, brightest morn, 
forerunner of the heavenly Sun of 
justice, Who didst first bring light 
to earth. 

Hail Mary, etc. 

4. We hail thee, O chosen one! 
who, like the untarnished sun, didst 
burst forth into being in the dark 
night of sin. 

Bail Mary, etc.

GLORY OF MARY IN DEATH. 
She was well prepared to die. 


Consider how glorious Mary was at 
the hour of her death, because in life 
she was so well prepared to die, by her 
most ardent longing to see her God 
and be forever united to her, Son, and 
by the unapproachable merit of her con- 
summate perfection. Then, consider- 
ing how different we are from Mary in 
Our preparation for death, we shall thus 
address her : 


od fare Virgin, who, to prepare 
thyself for a holy death, didst 
live in ceaseless longing for the vision 
of thy God, oh! do thou, virgin 
Mother! take from us the vain desire 
of the frail things of earth. 

Hail Mary, ¢hree times. 

Holiest Virgin, who, to prepare 
thyself to die holily, didst in life 
sigh to be united forever to thy Son 
Jesus, oh! obtain for us fidelity to 
Jesus even unto death. 

Hail Mary, three times.

of God alone are the road that leads 
to salvation.

GLORY OF MARY AFTER DEATH. 
In the resurrection of her body. 


Consider how glorious Mary was 
after death, since by the power of the 
Most High her sacred body, raised to 
life, acquired in an instant the gifts of 
brightness, subtlety, agility, and im- 
passibility ; and, filled with consolation 
at the excellence of her surpassing 
glory, let us thus invoke her: 

Aby exalted, who by thy God 

wast so gloriously raised to life, 

help us so to live on earth that we, 

too, like thee, may rise again on the 
last judgment day.

may be adorned with holy humility.

Wovena for the Feast of the 
Presentation. 


(Feast, Nov. 21st. Novena begins Nov. 
12th and ends Nov. 20th.) 


Recite daily nine times the Hail 
Mary; then say the following prayer: 
BELOVED Mother of God, most 
amiable child Mary, thou didst 
present thyself in tne Temple, and 
with promptitude and without reserve 
didst consecrate thyself to the glory 
and love of God; could I but offer 
thee this day the first years of my 
life, to devote myself without re- 
serve to thy service, my holy and 
most sweet Lady! But it is now 
too late to do this; for, unfortunate 
creature that I am, I have lost 
many years in the service of the 
world and my own caprices, and 
have lived in almost entire forget- 


£94 ' NOVENA FOR 


fulness of thee and of God. ‘‘ Woe 
to that time in which I did not love 
thee!’’ But it is better to begin late 
than not at all. Behold, O Mary, I 
this day present myself to thee, and 
I offer myself without reserve to thy 
service for the long or short time 
that I still have to live in this world; 
and, in union with thee, I renounce 
all creatures, and devote myself en- 
tirely to the love of my Creator. [| 
consecrate my mind to thee, O 
Queen! that it may always dwell on 
the love that thou deservest; my 
tongue, that it may praise thee; my 
heart, that it may love thee. Do 
thou accept, O most holy Virgin! 
the offering which this miserable 
sinner now makes thee; accept 
it, I beseech thee, by the consola- 
tion that thy heart experienced when 
thou didst give thyself to God in the 
Temple. But, since I enter thy ser- 
Vice late, it is reasonable that I 
should redouble my acts of homage 
and love, thereby to compensate for 
lost time. Do thou help my weak- 
ness with thy powerful intercession, 
Q Mother of mercy! by obtaining 


FESTIVALS OF THE BLESSED VIRGIN. 195 


me perseverance from thy Jesus and 
strength to be always faithful to thee 
until death, that, thus always serving 
thee in life, I may praise thee in 
paradise for all-eternity. Amen. 


For making a novena in honor of the 
presentation of Our Lady in the Temple 
an indulgence is granted of 300 days 
each day, and a plenary indulgence 
during the course of the novena or on 
one of the eight days immediately fol- 
lowing it.—Pius IX., Jan. 5, 1849, and 
Jan. 28, 1850.

SALUTATION TO THE BLESSED VIRGIN. 


SALUTE thee, O  ever-blessed 
Virgin, Mother of God, 

abyss of grace, miracle of almighty 
power! Isalute thee, O sanctuary of 
the Most Holy Trinity and Queen 
of the universe, Mother of mercy 
and assured refuge of sinners! O 
ever-faithful Virgin, ever-arniable 
and admirable Virgin! attracted by 
thy beauty and sweetness, by the 
odor of thy sanctity, the plenitude 
of thy graces, and the abundance of 
thy mercies, I presume to address 


196 NOVENA FOR 


thee, miserable as I am, and beseech 
thee to obtain for me of thy dear 
Son the grant of the petitions I 
present thee in this novena. [Here 
specify your requests.| Obtain for me 
likewise, O Queen of heaven! the 
most lively contrition for my mul- 
tiplied sins, and the grace to imi- 
tate faithfully those virtues which 
were exemplified in thy sacred life, 
particularly thy profound humility, 
angelic purity, and perfect obedience. 
Above all, I beseech thee to be my 
mother and protectress, to receive 
me into the number of thine adopted 
children, and to guide, conduct, and 
govern me from that high throne of 
glory to which thou art now exalted. 
Vouchsafe to hear my sighs and my 
desires ; reject not my petitions, O 
Mother of mercy! and let it not be 
said that of all who had recourse to 
thee I was the first thou didst ever 
forsake. O most amiable, pure, 
and immaculate Virgin! have com- 
passion on me, and do not abandon 
me during life or at the awful 
moment of my death. 


FESTIVALS OF THE BLESSED VIRGIN. 197 


Repeat the following salutation 
twelve times, and add, Glory be to 
the Father, etc., to every third repe- 
tition. - 

Hail, daughter of the eternal 
Father! Hail, Mother of the eter- 
nal Son! Hail, spouse of the most 
Holy Spirit! Hail, temple of the 
adorable Trinity ! 

O most tender heart of Mary, 
asylum of the poor and solid hope 
of sinners! penetrated with the most 
lively respect, love, and gratitude, I 
devote myself irrevocably to thy 
service, and offer thee my heart 
with all that I am and all belongs 
to me. 

Accept this offering, august Queen 
of heaven and earth, and obtain for 
me of thy dear Son, Jesus Christ, the 
favors I have presumed to demand 
through thy intercession in this 
novena, together with a tender, gen- 
erous, constant love of God, perfect’ 
submission to His ever-adorable will, 
the true spirit of a Christian, and 
the grace of final perseverance. 
Amen.

A PRAYER TO THE BLESSED VIRGIN. 


vHou most holy Virgin Mary, 

who dost evermore stand be- 
fore the throne of the Most Holy 
Trinity, and to whom it is granted at 
all times to pray for us to thy most 
beloved Son, pray for me in all my 
necessities ; help me, combat for me, 
give thanks for me, and obtain for 
me the pardon of all my sins. Help 
me especially at my last hour; and 
when I can no longer give any sign 
of the use of reason, then do thou 
encourage me, make the sign of the 
cross for me, sprinkle me with holy 
water, and fight for me against the 
enemy. Make in my name a pro- 
fession of faith, favor me with a 
testimony of my salvation, and never 
let me despair of the mercy of God. 
Help me to overcome the wicked 
enemy; and when I can no longer 
say, ‘‘Jesus, Mary, and Joseph, I 
place my soul in your hands,”’ say it 
for me; and when I can no longer 
hear human words of consolation, 
then do thou comfort me. Leave me 
not before I have been judged; and 


IMMACULATE HEART OF MARY. IyQ 


if I have to expiate my sins in purga- 
tory, oh! pray for me intently, ear- 
nestly, and admonish my friends to 
procure for me a speedy enjoyment 
of the blessed sight of God. Lessen 
my sufferings, deliver me speedily, 
and lead my soul into heaven with 
thee, that, united with all the elect, I 
may there bless and praise my God 
and thyself for all eternity. Amen. 
Three Hail Marys.

PRAYER. 

ae of Mary, Mother of God, 

our mother; heart most ami- 
able, delight of the ever-adorable 
Trinity, and worthy of all the ven- 
eration and tenderness of angels and 
men; heart most like the heart of 
Jesus, Whose perfect image thou art ; 
heart full of goodness, ever com- 
passionate towards our miseries ! 
vouchsafe to thaw our icy hearts, and 
change them to the likeness of the 
heart of Jesus. Infuse into them the 


200 NOVENA IN HONOR OF THE 


love of thy virtues; inflame them with 
that blessed fire with which thou dost 
ever burn. In thee let the holy 
Church find safe shelter ; be thou its 
guardian and its ever-sweet asylum, 
its tower of strength, impregnable 
against the assaults of its enemies. 
Be thou the road leading to Jesus ; 
be thou the channel whereby we re- 
ceive all graces needful for our sal- 
vation. Be thou our help in need, 
our comfort in trouble, our strength 
in temptation, our refuge in persecu- 
tion, our aid in danger; but espe- 
cially in the last struggle of our life, 
at the moment of our death, when 
all hell shall be unchained against us 
to snatch away our souls—in that 
dread moment, that hour so terrible, 
on which depends our eternity, ah! 
then, most tender Virgin, do thou 
make us feel how great is the sweet- 
ness of thy mother’s heart, how 
great thy power with the heart of 
Jesus, opening to us in the very fount 
of mercy itself a safe refuge, that so 
one day we too may join thee in 
paradise in praising the heart of 
Jesus forever and ever. Amen. 


IMMACULATE HEART OF MARY. 201 


May the divine heart of Jesus and 
the immaculate heart of Mary be 
known, praised, blessed, loved, wor- 
shipped, and glorified always and in 
all places. Amen. 


1. Indulgence of 60 days once a day. 

2. A plenary indulgence on the feasts 
of the Nativity. of the Assumption, and 
of the Immaculate Heart of Mary to all 
those who shall have said the above 
prayer every day during the year 
when, being truly penitent, after con- 
fession and communion, they shall visit 
a church or an altar in any church 
dedicated to the Blessed Virgin Mary 
and pray for the intention of the 
Sovereign Pontiff. 

3. A plenary indulgence at the hour 
of death to those who during life shall 
have practised this pious exercise 
every day,

PRAYER TO OUR DESOLATE MOTHER, 


O MOST desolate of mothers, what 

sword most terrible hath pene- 
trated thy soul! Each blow which 
Jesus received in His passion reached 
thy heart. Ah! His sorrows de- 
pressed thee, especially His last 
words from the cross, O Mother of 
grief and love! I come to entreat 
thy intercession, through thy griefs 
and sorrows, to obtain for me the 
intention of this rovena. [Here name 
your request.| Remember, O Queen 
of heaven and earth, that thou art the 
help of Christians, and refuse not to 
aid me in my present necessities. O 
Mary, conceived without sin, pray 
for us who have recourse to thee. 
Amen.

HOLY VIRGIN. 


ost glorious Virgin Mary, 
Mother of God and our 


210 NOVENA TO 


Mother, cast a glance of pity upon 
us, poor sinners, who, afflicted by the 
many evils which surround us in this 
life, feel our hearts wounded by the 
atrocities, injuries, and blasphemies 
which we are frequently compelled to 
hear pronounced against thee, im- 
maculate Virgin. Oh, how offen- 
sive is such language to the infinite 
majesty of God and to His only 
Son, Jesus Christ! how it provokes 
His wrath, and causes us to fear the 
terrible effects of His vengeance! 
If the sacrifice of our life would 
avail anything to prevent such out- 
rages and blasphemies, gladly would 
we Offer it; for, most holy Mother, 
we desire to love and honor thee 
with all our hearts, such being the 
will of God. Because we do love 
thee we will do whatever is in our 
power that thou mayest be loved 
and honored by all. Do thou mean. 
while, Mother full of pity, sovereign 
consoler of the afflicted, accept this 
act of reparation which we offer thee 
in our own names, and in those of 
all our families, even of such as, not 
«knowing what they say, impiously

blaspheme thee. By obtaining their 
conversion from God render yet more 
manifest and glorious thy pity, thy 
power, and thy great mercy, and 
they will unite with us in proclaim- 
ing thee blessed among women, the 
immaculate Virgin and the merciful 
Mother of God. Amen.

For making a novena in honor cx 
the dolors of Mary an indulgence is 
granted of 300 days on each day, and 
a plenary indulgence during the novena 
or on one of the days immediately fol- 
lowing it.—Pius IX., Jan. 5, 1849. 


Wovena to Our Lady, Queen of 
Martyrs, 
PRAYER, 
O most holy and afflicted Virgin, 
Queen of martyrs! thou who 
didst stand motionless beneath the 
cross, witnessing the agony of thy ex- 
piring Son: through the sword of grief 
which pierced thee then, through 
the unceasing suffering of thy life of 
sorrow, and the bliss which now 
more than amply repays thee for thy 


212 NOVENA TO 


past trials, look down with a mothev’ 
pity and tenderness on me who kner ¢ 
before thee te venerate thy sacred 
dolors and to place thy requests 
with filial confidence in the sanctuary 
of thy wounded heart. Present, I 
‘beseech thee, on my behalf to Jesus 
Christ the merits of His own most 
sacred death and passion, together 
with thy sufferings at the foc’ of the 
cross, and through the united efficacy 
of both obtain the grant of my 
present petitions. 

To whom shall I recur in my wants 
and miseries if not to thee, O Mother 
of mercy who, having drunk so deep- 
ly of the chalice of thy Son, canst 
compassionate the woes of those who 
are still doomed to sigh in this land 
of exile? Offer for me to my Say- 
iour one drop of the blood which 
flowed from His sacred veins, one of 
the tears which trickled from His 
divine eyes, one of the pangs which 
rent His adorable heart. 

O refuge of sinners and hope of 
the whole world, do not reject my 
earnest prayer, but graciously obtain 
the grant of my requests. Amen.

Wovena to Our Lady of Perpetual 
Delp. 


(Feast, Sunday before June 24th.) 


INSTRUCTION. 


HERE is in the church of St. Alphon. 

sus in Rome a curious and well. 
known picture of the Blessed Virgin; it 
is very old, as can be seen uponclose ex- 
amination. Its origin is unknown, but 
it has been venerated in the Eternal City 
for several centuries. Devout Catholics 
aking pilgrimages to the holy shrine 
have received many striking graces, so 
that in course of time it became known 
‘arand near. There is hardly a pious 
household that does not possess a copy 
of this celebrated pic.are, so that a de. 
votion to Our Lady of Perpetual Helr 
has sprung up and is constantly in. 
creasing. By this devotion Mary has 
inspired us with a confidence in her con. 
stant willingness to assist us in all om 
trials and difficulties. Herearsare con 
stantly attentive to our supplicaticns 
and she adds her own prayers to their 
to make them mcre effective. Mary 
certainly will not herself be the irspire 
of this confidence and then uot be ar 
efficient helper. When cur friends are 
in trouble, do we not say to them, 
‘*Why do you nottrythis ovthat? Per. 
haps you will find reJief.” Mary, the 
Mother of God, ann by that fact our 


214 NOVENA TO 


mother also, says the same to us: ‘‘ Why 
do you not address yourself tome? I 
am always ready to help you and te 
intercede in your behalf with my divine 
Son, Jesus Christ.” Very many cures 
and conversions have taken place 
through the devotion to this picture. 

To make, this devotion, procure a 
picture of the Mother of Perpetual 
Help; make a little altar in your home 
with candles at the side of the picture 
and other ornaments about it. At cer- 
tain times light these candles; go to 
Mass every morning if possible; at the 
beginning and conclusion of the devo- 
tion go to confession and communion, 
recite every day the following prayers, 
the Litany of the Blessed Virgin, and 
the Rosary; in short, say as many 
prayers in honor of the Blessed Virgin 
as your circumstances will permit. 


First Day. 


Ml" confidence I approach this 
holy picture so favored by 
thee, that I may obtain the grace 
necessary for my salvation. This is 
my very first wish, to be freed from 
the stuin of sin and to return to God, 
my Favher. By what way can I 
more readily expect to reach my end 
than threugh thee, the Mother of 
His divine Son? My sins are very 


OUR LADY OF PERPETUAL HELP. 215 


great and very numerous, and I do 
not deserve any hearing, but rather 
to be spurned from thy presence. 
Still I will come to thee and visit 
thee in this thy picture, and wil? 
make this nine days’ devotion. 1 
can in this way atone a little for my 
past, and thou wilt certainly look 
with favor on me when thou seest 
the sincerity of my heart and my 
willingness to serve God in the 
future. This I promise, and am sure 
that with thy help the obstacles to 
my conversion will be removed and 
I will be again a beloved child of 
God, my Father. 
The Litany and the Rosary. 


Second Day. 


6° thee, O Mother, I come, that 
thou mayst inspire me with 
great confidence in thy power and 
willingness to help. I am thy child, 
because thou hast chosen me ; brought 
up in the Church, I have always been 
taught to have reverence for the 
Mother of God. I prayed to thee 
when my childish mind hardly knew 
what this devotion meant; but as 


216 NOVENA TU 


years have gone by I have under- 
stood better what the Church herself 
has taught me, to pray fervently, be- 
cause if it be the will of God my 
prayer will surely be heard. In all 
trouble, in pain, and in misfortune 
of every kind I have looked up to 
thee, O kind Mother, and felt that 
the mantle of thy goodness was 
covering me; what I suffered was 
my deserts, to bring me to my senses, 
knowing full well at the same time 
that many other misfortunes were 
withheld from me through thy in- 
tercession and protection. 
Litany and Rosary. 


Tuiro Day. 


Am thine own child, dear 
Mother, When thy divine Son 
died on the cross thou didst adopt 


_ all the future members of the Church 


as thy children, and_ especially 
wert thou a tender mother to the 
poor sinners that caused the death 
of thy Son. I trust, therefore, that 
the voice of thy child will be heard 
by thee, tender Mother. Shall the 
infant cry, and the mother turn a 


OUR LADY OF PERPETUAL HELP. 217 


deaf ear to its wailings? ‘Thou wilt 
hasten to my aid with all that is 
necessary for my relief, for my help. 
To thee, O Mother, I send up my 
sighs ; assist thy child, so weak that 
it is not able to help itself. What- 
ever difficulties I have, there is one 
to whom I can go and lay before her 
my necessities; thou understandest 
my condition better than I do my- 
self. Even whilst I pray selfishness 
and pride pervade my prayer and 
my requests ; but do thou purify my 
heart from all these vices, so that 
God may have mercy on me and by 
thy powerful intercession be induced 
to give thee the means of alleviating 
my conditicn by thy own hands. 
Litany and Rosary. 


FourtH Day. 


HOLY Mary, I again come be- 

fore thee. I have already 
made known my poverty. I am 
nething but sins, for I am full of 
them, and vices, which still exist in 
me. Look with pity on me. What- 
ever thou shalt give, I will be the 
richer for it. I sholl always be poor 


218 NOVENA TO 


and miserable unless thou helpest to 
take away my sins; I shall remain a 
sinner unless thou enrichest my soul 
with virtues. I appeal to all the 
sufferings of thy divine Son ; there 
on Calvary He died on the cross, 
and surely His death must not go in 
vain. Thou wert nearest to Him in 
His sufferings, thou art nearest to 
Him now, and thou wilt remember 
those sufferings and offer them to 
God as an atonement for my wretched 
state. All my troubles are caused 
by my sins; all the sufferings ef 
Christ were inflicted by my sins. 
Through thy intercession I hope for 
salvation, pardon from Our Lord, and 
help in my present condition. 
Litany and Rosary. 


Firtu Day. 


O MotTuHeR of Perpetual Help! 

while I pray for the intention 
of this novena and recommend to thee 
my concerns in order that they may 
be relieved, at the same time I beg of 
thee, dear Mother, the grace of per- 
severing in the pursuit of virtue. At 
thy feet t have learned the virtues of 


OUR LADY OF PERPETUAL HELP. 219 


purity, humility, meekness, and zeal 
for the glory of God. Give me 
grace now to put them in practice, 
so that in my frailty I may not again 
fall into the vices which I have ac- 
knowledged. I wish, O dear Mother, 
to deserve thy kindness by the prac- 
tice of virtue. I do not want the 
favors for which I make this novena 
unless J have been faithful, and thou 
wishest to reward me for my good 
works, especially my confidence in 
thee. 
Litany and Rosary. 


SrxtH Day. 


DEAREST Mother Mary! behoid 

me prostrate before thee, 

with a heart wounded by sin. I 
have been wilfully sinful, purposely 
bad, in order to enjoy the evil in- 
clinations of my passions. I cannot 
claim ignorance; I have done ail 
this with a full knowledge of the con- 
sequences of my sins ; and even now, 
while I am praying to thee, I am in 
the same condition. I have no right 
to be heard. Still thou art so good 
a mother that thou wilt make every 


220 NOVENA TO 


effort, according to the will of God, 
to obtain for me the favors I now 
implore. I will do penance for my 
sins ; and here, as an act of mortifi- 
cation, I offer thee this promise: to 
love my neighbor under all circum- 
stances. 
Litany and Rosary. 


SEVENTH Day. 


Mma of grace ! on'tnis seventh 
day of my novena I find that 
I have increased in fervor and piety, 
and the object for which I pray has be- 
come dearer and dearer to my heart. 
I have bewailed my sins and imper- 
fections and reflected on them, and 
with the grace of God I have made 
firm resolutions to make amends. I 
hope, therefore, if it be the will of 
Almighty God, that thou wilt listen 
to my prayers in my present necessi- 
ties. Why art thou called Mother 
of Perpetual Help if not that at 
every moment we can have recourse 
to thee and find relief in ail our 
troubles ? 
Litany and Rosary. 


OUR LADY OF PERPETUAL HELP. 221 


EIGHTH Day. 


O MoTHER most pure! I wish to 
beg of thee the strength to 
conquer temptations. When I go 
back into the turmoil of the world 
I will feel as if I had left a beautiful 
home, where my Mother dwells, 
I will feel as if left to myself, with 
no one to encourage me in the 
temptations of this world—tempta- 
tions so much the more dangerous 
because I have not character enough 
to stand up nobly and refuse the 
pleasures that my passions promise to 
give me. This is a fearful] struggle. 
and for humanity, unless aided by 
supernatural help, almost hopeless. 
Oh! then be to mea shield against 
'the assaults of the devil, and help me 
to avoid the occasions of sin. Stand 
by my side, O my Mother, in all my 
temptations. 
Litany and Rosary. 


Ninty Day. 


O Mary, Queen of the heavenly 
court! who standest beside 
the throne of thy divine Son, who 


622 NOVENA TO 


art greater than the angels and 
saints, and art now in the enjoyment 
of eternal bliss, look down on me, 
thy child, in this vale of tears ; help 
me to come to God, my last end, to 
come to Jesus, Who is my Redeemer. 
Thou hast received the title of 
Mother of Perpetual Help, and thou 
hast shown thy approval by granting 
so many graces and favors under that 
title. Thou art now in heaven and 
all-powerful with God. Stretch forth 
thy hand and help me to a happy 
eternity. Grant that every day i 
may arise with renewed courage and 
fervor to do that which is most use- 
ful to my neighbor and the honor of 
God. 


MEMORARE., 


EMEMBER, O most pious Virgin 
Mary! that no one ever had 
recourse to thy protection or sought 
thy mediation without obtaining re- 
lief. Confiding, then, in thy good- 
ness and mercy, I cast myself at thy 
sacred feet, and do most humbly 
supplicate thee, O- Mother of the 
Eternal Word, to adopt me as thy

child and to take upon thyself the care 
of my eternal salvation. Oh! let it 
not be said, dearest Mother, that 1 
have perished at thy sacred feet, 
where no one has ever found but 
mercy, grace, and salvation.

Triduum in ibonor of Our Lady of 
Perpetual help. 


First Tay. 


| Bee at thy feet, O Mother of 
Perpetual Help! a wretched 
sinner who has recourse to thee and 
confides in thee. O Mother of 
mercy! have pity on me. I hear 
thee called by all the refuge and 
the hope of sinners: be, then, my 
refuge and my hope. Assist me, 
for the love of Jesus Christ ; stretch 
forth thy hand to a miserable fallen 
creature who recommends himself to 
thee, and who devotes himself to thy 
service forever. I bless and thank 
Almighty God, Who in mercy has 
given me this confidence in thee, 
which I hold to be a pledge of my 
eternal salvation. It is true that in 
the past I have miserably fallen into 
“in, because I had not recourse to 
hee. I know that with thy help I 


OUR LADY OF PERPETUAL HELP. 22g 


shall conquer. J know, too, that 
thou wilt assist me if I recommend 
myself to thee; but I fear that in 
time of danger I may neglect to call 
on thee, and thus lose my soul. This 
grace, then, I ask of thee, and this I 
beg with all the fervor of my soul, 
that in all the attacks of hell I may 
ever have recourse to thee. O Mary! 
help me. O Mother of Perpetual 
Help, never suffer me to lose my 
God. 


Seconp Day. 


O MoruHer of Perpetual Help! 

grant that I may ever invoke 
thy most powerful name, which is the 
safeguard of the living and the sal- 
vation of the dying. O purest Mary ! 
© cweetest Mary! let thy name 
henceforth be ever on my lips. De- 
lay not, O blessed Lady! to succor 
me whenever I call on thee ; for in 
all my temptations, in all my needs, 
I shall never cease to call on thee, 
ever repeating thy sacred name, 
Mary, Mary. Oh, what consolation, 
what sweetness, what confidence, 
wnat emotion fills my soul when I 


230 TRIDUUM IN HONOR OF 


utter thy sacred name, or even only 
think of thee! I thank the Lord for 
having given thee, for my good, so 
sweet, so powerful, so lovely a name. 
But I will not be content with merely 
uttering Thy name. Let my love 
for thee prompt me ever to hail thee, 
Mother of Perpetual Help. 


Tuirp Day. 


O MotHeErR of Perpetual Help! 

thou art the dispenser of all 
the gifts which God grants to us 
miserable sinners ; and for this end 
He has made thee so powerful, so 
rich, and so bountiful, in order that 
thou mayest succor us in our misery. 
Thou art the advocate of the most 
wretched and abandoned sinners who 
have recourse to thee: come to my 
help, I commend myseif to thee. 
In thy hands I place my eternal sal- 
vation, and to thee I entrust my soul. 
Count me among thy most devoted 
servants; take me under thy pro- 
tection, and it is enough for me, 
for if thou protect me I fear 
nothing: not from my sins, because 
thou wilt obtain for me the pardon 


CUR LADY OF PERPETUAL HELP 23) 


of them ; nor from the devils, because 
thou art more powerful than all hell 
together; nor even from Jesus, my 
Judge, because by one prayer from 
thee He will be appeased. But one 
thing I fear, that in the hour of 
temptation, I may, through negli- 
gence, fail to have recourse to thee, 
and thus perish miserably. Obtain 
for me, therefore, the pardon of my 
sins, love for Jesus, final perse- 
verance, and the grace ever to have 
recourse to thee, O Mother of Per- 
petual Help !

for each prayer.—Pius IX., May 17,

divine Son. Amen.

PRAYER FOR COUNSEL AND

M°*: holy Virgin, who wast 

chosen to be the Mother of the 
Incarnate Word, and who thus didst 
become the seat of wisdom, the 
Mother of good counsel, and the faith- 
ful dispenser of heavenly gifts to us, 
thy adopted children, we supplicate 
thee to enlighten our understanding, 
to direct our steps, to console us by 
thy maternal protection, so that, ex- 
empt from all error, sheltered from 
every danger, strengthened against 
our enemies, we may with ardor and 
invincible courage walk in the paths 
traced out for us by thee and thy 
divine Son. We do not deserve so 
great a favor, but we know that thou 
art our Mother, and dost not measure 
thy graces according to our merits.

but according to the extent of thy 
power and the generosity of thy 
maternal heart. Filled with this 
confidence, we are assured that thy 
protection will never fail us, and we 
hope to thank thee in heaven, singing 
thy praises forever. Amen,
