Rovena 19 Our Lady of the Rosary 
of Pompeii. 


INSTRUCTION. 


BOUT twenty years ago there 
lived in the south of Italy a 
awyer, Signor Bartolo Longo, who, far 
from being a saint, had forsaken his 
religion and indulged in spiritualism 
and magnetism. But during the month 
of October, 1872, he was miraculously 
struck by divine grace on the very spot 
on which afterwards arose the sanctu-~ 
ary of Our Lady of Pompeii. 
One day he was wandering alone in 
a wild spot in the valley of Pompeii, 
meditating on the best way to atone for 
his sins, to find again the peace and 
happiness of the days when he was 
still true to his religion, when suddenly 
he heard a sweet voice saying to him: 
‘Wilt thou find peace again? Spread 
my Rosary, for whoever propagates the 
uevotion of the Rosary shall never per- 


244 NOVENA TO OUR LADY 


ish.’”’ He answered: ‘‘If thy promise 
is sure, I am saved, and I am happy 
once more, for I shall spread thy 
Rosary.” 

He set about to spread the devotion 
to the holy Rosary of Our Lady of Pom- 
peii, and in less than twenty years, on 
the very spot where he had heard the 
voice, there stood, commenced by poor 
peasants, finished, thanks to the offer- 
ings of the entire world, the sanctuary 
which Pope Leo XIII. has taken under 
his special patronage. Thousands of 
pilgrims from all parts of the world go 
there every year to venerate the picture 
of Our Ladv which is there, framed in 
gold and precious stones. The Holy 
Father has attached numerous privi- 
leges to this sanctuary, and many won- 
derful graces have been obtained and 
many miracles performed. 


Nobena of Entpetration. 


Put the miraculous picture of the 
Virgin of Pompeii in a becoming place 
. and, if possible, light before it two 
candles as asymbol of your faith and 
fervor, and then pray as follows: 


Es the name of the Father, and 
“ of the Son, and of the Holy 
‘Ghost. Amen.

O IMMACULATE Virgin and Queen 
of the holy Rosary, in these 
days of dying faith and triumphant 
impiety thou hast deigned to set 
thy throne of Queen and Mother on 
the ancient land of Pompeii, of yore 
the abode of heathens; and from the 
spot where idols and demons were 
worshipped thou, as the Mother of 
divine grace, dost now lavish on this 
world the treasures of the heavenly 
mercies. From tha“ throne where 
thou reignest so mercifully, O Mary, 
turn down thy pitiful eyes towards 
me too, and have mercy on me, 
who am in such need of thy help. 
Show thyself to me also, as thou hast 
shown thyself to so many, a true 
Mother of mercy: monsira fe esse 
malrem ; whilst I, from the bottom 
of my heart, greet and invoke thee 
as my sovereign and Queen of the 
most holy Rosary.

py he who consecrates to thee his 
love. Art thou not, O Blessed Vir-

lily in splendor? Art thou not a 
perfumed flower, a fair dove, the be- 
loved of Jesus, the cherished daugh- 
ter, the mother without spot, the 
“pouse chosen among a thousand? 
Who, then, can refuse thee his heart ? 
I give thee, then, mine, because 
thou art fair, enriched with every 
grace, and filled with every perfec- 
tion. I give thee, then, mine, O 
Mary, because gratitude urges me to

thee, and in pronouncing perfect acts 
of love; 3d, the grace to be among 
_the privileged choir who in heaven, 
at the foot of thy throne, will sing 
thy praises forever. 

O Mother of God and my most 
dear Mother, by the heart of Jesus 
I beg of thee each time I shall say,

to adore then for me the heart of

God of love how much it desires per- 
fectly to love Him; and obtain by 
thy powerful intercession a love that 
may consume in it all earthly affec- 
tions, and that He may replenish it to 
the full extent of its small capacity.

Thirty Days’ Prayer to the Blessed 
Virgin. 


VER-GLORIOUS and blessed Mary, 
Queen of virgins, Mother of 
mercy, hope and comfort of dejected 
and desolate souls, through that 
sword of sorrow which pierced thy 
tender heart while thine only Son, 
Christ Jesus, Our Lord, suffered death 
and ignominy on the cross; through 
that filial tenderness and pure love 
He had for thee, grieving in thy 
grief, while from the cross He recom- 
mended thee to the care and protec-~ 
tion of His beloved disciple, St. 
John—take pity, I beseech thee, on 
my poverty and necessities; have 
compassion on my anxieties and 
cares; assist and comfort me in all 
my infirmities and miseries of what 
kind soever. ‘Thou art the mother 
of mercies, the sweet consolatrix and 
only refuge of the needy and the or- 
phan, of the desolate and the afflicted. 


TO THE BLESSED VIKGIN. 26035 


Cast, therefore, an eye of pity ona 
miserable, forlorn child of Eve, and 
hear my prayer; for since, in just 
punishment for my sins, I find my- 
self encompassed by a multitude of 
evils and oppressed with much an- 
guish of spirit, whither can I fly for 
more secure shelter, O amiable 
Mother of my Lord and Saviour 
Jesus Christ, than under the wings ot 
thy maternal protection? Attend, 
therefore, I beseech thee, with an 
ear of pity and compassion to my 
humble and earnest request. I ask 
it through the wonderful mercy of thy 
dear Son, through that love and 
condescension wherewith He em- 
braced our nature when, in compli- 
ance with the divine will, thou gav- 
est thy consent, and Whom after the 
expiration of nine months thou 
didst bring forth from-the chaste en- 
closure of thy womb, to visit this 
world and bless it with His presence. 
I askit through that anguish of mind 
wherewith thy beloved Son, our dear 
Saviour, was overwhelmed on Mount 
Olivet when He besought His 
eternal Father to remove from Him,

appearance for forty days after gave 
thee, His blessed Mother, His apos- 
tles, and the rest of His disciples, 
when in thine and their presence He 
miraculously ascended into heaven. I 
ask it through the grace of the Holy 
Ghost infused into the hearts of the 
disciples when He descended upon

ask it through the awful appearance

thy suppliant by obtaining for me 
[here name your request}; and as I

{ndulgence of 100 days every time. 
Plenary indulgence on the feast.—Pius 
VI, Oct.2, 1795: 

A devotion to the holy angels agree- 
able to Our Lord is an exercise of 
nine acts of mortification, or nine Hail 
Marys, ornine genuflections before the 
Blessed Sacrament, in honor of the nine 
choirs of angels. 

For making a novena to the hely 
guardian angels an indulgence of 100 
days, once a day, and a plenary indul- 
gence during the novena or the eight 
days following it.—Pius IX., Nov. 26, 
1876.

For making a novena to St. Michael 
an indulgence of 300 days each day, 
and a plenary indulgence either during 
the novena or on one of the eight days 
immediately following it.—Pius IX.,

fovena to Our Patron Saint, 


INSTRUCTION. 


6° every Catholic the patron saint is 

avery important personage for 
his whole life. The name of some saint 
is given to the child, and the knowl- 
edge of this saint and the name seem 
to have a great deal of influence on the 
after life of the child. Our patron 
saint should bea very particular friend 
of ours ; we should know on what day 
his feast occurs, that we may be able to 
celebrate it with a novena. We should 
xnow the life of this saint, so as to be 
able to imitate his virtues. Our patron 
saint is a friend of God, full of the love 


NOVENA TO OUR PATRON SAINT. 2q. 


of God and His glory; he is in heaven 
enjoying an eternity of bliss which will 
be his forever. Under the invocation 
of this saint we were baptized and 
consecrated to the service of the 
Almighty. Churches and institutions 
are also named in honor of some saint, 
that they may have him as a protector, 
These saints have an obligation to look 
after their charges; they are not om. 
niscient, but in the omnipresence of 
God they can see what is needed by 
their foster-children on earth. Our 
prayers enter into the very heart o1 
God, and the saints who continually 
contemplate God’s goodness see the 
prayers ascending, and they beg the 
Father of mercy for grace for those 
who need it, and help for those who are 
detached from the things of this world. 
We must be sure not to honor a saint, 
to pray to him, or make a novena, sim- 
ply for the purpose of gaining some 
_temporal advantage ; no, we must think 
first of the kingdom of God, of our own 
Spiritual good and that of our neighbor. 
We ought to look more to the imitation 
of the virtues in the lives of the saints, 
especially our own patron saint, than 
saying many prayers to them. 


First Day. 


O HOLY patron, St. ,» I am 
about to make this novena 


in thy honor and for my spirituai 


392 NOVENA TO OUR PATRON SAINT. 


senefit. During the coming nine 
days I will thank Almighty God for 
the many graces He has given thee. 
Thou hast been faithful to all thy 
duties, and therefore wert so pleasing 
co God that He became thy great 
friend. He added grace upon grace, 
and thou, O holy patron, wert more 
and more faithful to them, until thou 
didst attain eminent sanctity, and 
after thy glorious death didst obtain 
a high place in heaven. 

We should be friends ; I ought to 
know thee better, have more con- 
fidence in thee, be more familiar 
with thee, so that under all circum- 
stances I may quickly think of thee 
and take my refuge in thee ; then the 
purpose for which God intended thee 
will be more easily obtained, when 
I co-operate with God’s favors and 
graces. 

The Memorare.

DEDICATION TO ST. JOSEPH. 


O GLORIOUS descendant of the King 
of Juda! inheritor of the vir- 


NOVENA TO ST. JOSEPH. 299 


tues of all the patriarchs! just and 
happy St. Joseph! listen to my 
prayer. Thou art my glorious pro- 
tector, and shalt ever be, after Jesus 
and Mary, the object of my most 
profound veneration and tender con- 
fidence. Thou art the most hidden, 
though the greatest saint, and art 
peculiarly the patron of those who 
serve God with the greatest purity and 
fervor. In union with all those who 
have ever been most devoted to 
thee, I now dedicate myself to thy 
service, beseeching thee, for the 
sake of Jesus Christ, Who vouchsafed 
to love and obey thee as a son, to 
become a father to me, and to ob- 
tain for me the filial respect, confi- 
dence, and love of a child towards 
thee. O powerful advocate of all 
Christians! whose intercession, as 
St. Teresa assures us, has never been 
found to fail, deign to intercede for 
me now, and to implore for me the 
particular intention of this novena. 
[ Specify it.] Present me, O great 
saint, to the Adorable Trinity, with 
Whom thou hadst so glorious and 
so intimate a correspondence. Ob 


300 NOVENA TO ST. JOSEPH. 


tain that I may never. efface 
by sin the sacred image accord- 
ing to the likeness of which I was 
created. Beg for me that my divine 
Redeemer will enkindle in my 
heart, and in all hearts, the fire of 
His love, and infuse therein the vir- 
tues of His adorable infancy, His 
purity, simplicity, obedience, and 
humility. Obtain for me likewise a 
lively devotion to thy virgin spouse, 
and protect me so powerfully in life 
and death that I may have the hap- 
piness of dying as thou didst, in the 
friendship of my Creator, and under 
the immediate protection of the 
Mother of God.

GC St. Joseph, thou generous 

depositary and dispenser of im- 
mortal riches, behold us prostrate at 
thy feet, conjuring thee to receive us 
as thy servants and as thy children. 
Next to the sacred hearts of Jesus and 
Mary, of which thou art the f>*thful 
copy, we acknowledge that tuere is 
no heart more tender, more compas. 
sionate, than thine. What, then, have

we to fear, or, rather, for what should 
we not hope, if thou dost deign to 
be our benefactor, our master, our

erful protector! We ask it-6f he 
by the love thou hast for Jesus and 
Mary. Into thy hands we rétnit*ur 
souls and bodies, but above’ all the

after having honored, imitated, and

with thee the mercies of Jesus and 
Mary. Amen.

THE SEVEN SUNDAYS TO ST. JOSEPH. 305 


Devotion of the Seven Sundays in 
fbonor of St. Joseph. 


INSTRUCTION. 


HE Sovereign Pontiffs who have so 

gloriously occupied the chair of St. 
Peter in later days have enriched with 
great indulgences this tender devotion 
in order to stimulate the faithful to its 
practice. In virtue of a concession of 
Pope Gregory XVI. we may gain an 
indulgence of 300 days on each of the 
first six Sundays and a plenary indul- 
gence on the seventh Sunday for saying 
the prayers of the seven sorrows and 
joys of St. Joseph on any seven con- 
secutive Sundays of the year. 

On the 1st of February, 1347, Pope 
Pius IX., of holy memory, wishing to 
extend this devotion, added to the in- 
dulgences already granted a plenary 
indulgence on each Sunday, applicable 
to the souls in purgatory. 

On the 22d of March of the same 
year His Holiness extended this indul. 
gence to those who, owing to some 
good cause, are unable to recite the 
prayers of the seven sorrows and joys 
of St. Joseph, but who say seven Our 
Fathers and seven Hail Marys and 
seven Giorias, fulfilling the usual condi- 
tions for gaining a plenary indulgence, 
namely, confession, communion, and 
prayers for our holy mother the Church. 

The faithful servants of St. Joseph 


306 DEVOTION OF THE SEVEN SUNDAYS 


responded earnestly to the desires of 
the Vicar of Christ, and the precious 
graces and miracles obtained by this 
devotion have greatly increased the 
honor due to the holy patriarch. 

This devotion may be performea 
when we desire to obtain some great 
favor, or toward the feast of St. Joseph. 


DEVOUT EXERCISE IN HONOR OF THE 
SEVEN SORROWS AND SEVEN 
JOYS OF ST. JOSEPH. 


'¢ URE husband of most holy 
Mary, glorious St. Joseph, 
great was the travail and anguish of 
thy heart when, in sore perplexity, 
thou wast minded to put away thy 
stainless spouse ; but unspeakable was 
thy joy when the angel revealed to 
thee the high mystery of the incar- 
nation. 

By this thy sorrow and thy joy, we 
pray thee comfort our souls now 
and in their dying agony with the 
sweet consolation of a well-spent life, 
and a death like unto thine own, in 
the embrace of Jesus and of Mary. 

Our Father, Hail Mary, Glory be 
to the Father, etc. 

2. Thrice-happy patriarch, glori- 


IN HONOR OF cf. JOSEPH. 307 


ous St. Joseph, chosen to be the 
foster-father of the Word made man, 
keen was the pain thou didst feel 
when thou didst see the Infant Jesus 
born in abject poverty ; but thy pain 
was suddenly changed into heavenly 
joy when upon thee burst the har- 
mony of the angel choirs, and thou 
didst behold the glory of that reful- 
gent night. 

By this thy sorrow and thy joy, 
we pray thee obtain for us that, 
when the journey of our life is 
over, we too may pass to that blessed 
jand where we shall hear the angel 
chants and enjoy the brightness of 
eternal glory. 

Our Father, Hail Mary, Glory be 
to the Father, etc. 

3. O thou who wast ever most 
obedient in fulfilling the law of God,. 
glorious St. Joseph, when at His 
circumcision the Infant Saviour’s 
precious blood.was shed thy heart was 
pierced through and through; but 
with the name of Jesus came again 
to thee new life and heavenly joy. 

By this thy sorrow and thy joy, 
sotain for us that, freed in life 


308 DEVOTION OF THE SEVEN SUNDAYS 


from the vile yoke of sin, we too 
may die with joy, with the sweet 
name of Jesus in our hearts and on 
our lips. 

Our Father, Hail Mary, Glory be 
to the Father, etc. 

4. Faithful saint, who wast ad- 
mitted to take part in man’s redemp- 
tion, glorious St. Joseph, Simeon’s 
prophecy of the coming woes of 
Jesus and Mary filled thy soul with 
agony like death; but thy soul was 
filled with blessedness when he fore- 
told salvation and glorious resurrec- 
tion to innumerable souls. 

By this thy sorrow and thy joy. 
help us with thy prayers to be of 
those who, by the merits of Jesus and 
His Virgin Mother, shall be par- 
takers of the glorious resurrection. 

Our Father, Hail Mary, Glory be 
to the Father, etc. 

5. Watchful guardian, bosom- 
friend of the incarnate Son of God, 
glorious St. Joseph, how didst thou 
toil to nurture and to serve the Son 
of the Most High, especially in the 
flight into Egypt; but far greater 
was thy joy in having with thee God 


IN HONOR OF ST. JOSEPH. 309 


flimself, and in seeing Egypt’s idols 
fall to the earth. 

By this thy sorrow and thy joy, 
obiain for us to keep aloof from the 
infernal tyrant, quitting all dangerous 
occasions, that all earthly idols may 
be cast out from our hearts, and that, 
employed in the service of Jesus and 
Mary, we may ever live for them 
alone, and with them calmly die. 

Our Father, Hail Mary, Glory be 
to the Father, etc. 

6. Angel on earth, glorious St. 
Joseph, while thou didst marvel at 
seeing the King-of heaven obedient 
to thy bidding, fear of the tyrané 
mingled with thy joy when thou didst 
bring Him back from Egypt; but, 
reassured by the angel, thou didst 
dwell at Nazareth with glad heart, 
in the sweet company of Jesus and 
Mary. 

By this thy sorrow and thy joy, 
obtain for us that, with heart set free 
from every hurtful fear, we too may 
taste the quiet of a tranquil con- 
science, safely dwelling with Jesus 
and Mary, and one day die within 
their loving arms.

Our Father, Hail Mary, Glory be

7. Pattern of all holiness, glorious 
St. Joseph, without fault of thine 
thou didst lose the holy Child Jesus, 
- and for three days, to thy great sor- 
row, didst seek for Him until, with 
joy unspeakable, thou didst find thy 
Life amid the doctors in the Temple. 

By this thy sorrow and thy joy, 
we pray thee with all our heart 
stand between us and danger, that we 
may never lose Jesus by mortal sin ; 
but if, to our shame and disgrace, we 
lose Him, may we seek Him with 
such ceaseless grief that we may find 
Him propitious to us, especially at 
the hour of our death, and thus go to 
enjoy Him in heaven, and there with 
thee sing His divine mercy forever.

to the Father, etc.

fend the holy Church of God from

OLY St. Joseph, spouse of Mary, 

be mindful of me, pray for me, 
watch over me. Guardian of the 
paradise of the new Adam, provide

PRAYER. 


O GREAT and glorious patriarch, 
St. Joachim, how I rejoice 
in the thought that thou wast selected 
from among all the saints tu cu- 
operate in the divine mysterie, and 
to enrich the world with the great 
Mother of God, Mary most holy. 


NOVENA TO ST. JOACHIM, 317 


By this especial privilege thou didst 
become most powerful with the 
Mother and with the Son, so that 
there is no grace, however great, 
which thou canst not obtain. Ani- 
mated with such confidence, I have 
recourse to thy powerful protection, 
and recommend to thee all my needs 
and those of my family, both spiritual 
and temporal, but particularly the 
special grace which I desire and ex- 
pect from thy fatherly intercession. 
And as thou wert a perfect model of 
the interior life, obtain for me the 
grace of recollection and detachment 
from the fleeting goods of this earth, 
‘with a lively and persevering love 
for Jesus and Mary. Obtain for me 
also sincere devotion and obedience 
to holy Church and to the Sovereign 
Pontiff governing it, that I may live 
and die in faith, hope, and perfect 
charity, invoking the holy names of 
Jesus and Mary, and thus be saved. 
Amen.

Glory be to the Father.

PRAYER TO ST. ANNE, MOTHER OF 
THE BLESSED VIRGIN. 


a" a heart full of sincere filial 
veneration I prostrate myself 
before thee, O blessed St. Anne. 
Thou art that beloved and privileged 
creature who, because of thy ex- 
traordinary virtue and sanctity, didst 
deserve of God that chief of graces, 
of giving life to the treasury of 
grace, the blessed among women, 
the Mother of the incarnate Lord, 
the Blessed Virgin Mary. 

Ah! in consideration of such ex- 
alted favors deign, O most tender 
saint, to receive me among the num- 
ber of thy truly devoted servants, 
for such I protest myself to be and 
wish to remain for the rest of my 
life. Surround me with thy effica 
cious patronage, and obtain for me 
from God the imitation of those 
virtues with which thou wert so pro- 


NOVENA TO ST. ANNE. 319 


fusely adorned. Obtain for me a 
knowledge of my sins and sorrow 
for them, an ardent love for Jesus 
and Mary, a faithful and constant 
observance of the duties of my state 
of life. Save me from all dangers 
In life, and assist me at the hour of 
my death, that 1 may safely reach 
paradise, there to piaise with thee, 
most happy mother, the Word of 
God made mar in the womb of thy 
most pure daughter, the Blessed 
Virgin Mary. Amen.

to the Father, etc., zhree “mes.

so worthy, and so perfect a spouse ; 
the angels and the elect honor thee 
as the mother of their sovereign ; 
the just, the penitent, and the sinner 
consider thee their powerful advo- 
cate before God, for by thy inter- 
cession the just hope for an increase 
of grace, the penitent for their 
justification, and sinners for the re- 
mission of their sins. Be, then, kind 
and generous, and while we invoke 
thee here below pray for us in heaven. 
Use in our favor the great influence 
thou hast before God, and permit not 
that those who know thee be lost. 
Show thyself always the refuge of sin- 
ners and the asylum of the guilty, 
the consolation of the afflicted, and 
give us the pledge of thy kindness. 
Defend our cause now and at the 
hour of our death. ‘This we pray of 
thee by all the love which thou hast 
for Our Lord and for His Mother,

PRAYER TO ST. ANNE TO OBTAIN 
SOME SPECIAL FAVOR. 


Go St. Anne, filled with 

compassion for those who in- 
voké thee and with love for those 
who suffer, heavily laden with the 
weight of my troubles, I cast myself 
at thy feet and humbly beg of thee 
to take the present affair which I 
recommend to thee under thy special 
protection. | Vouchsafe to recom- 
mend it to thy daughter, the Blessed 
Virgin Mary, and lay it before the 
throne of Jesus, so that He may: 
bring it to a happy issue. Cease not 
to intercede for me until my request 
is granted. Above all, obtain for 
me the grace of one day beholding 
my God face to face, and with thee 
and Mary and all the saints praising 
and blessing Him through all eter- 
nity. Amen.

PRAYER FOR THE PRELATES AND 
PASTORS OF HOLY CHURCH. 


O BLESSED victim of charity, whose 
heart was consumed with the 
most ardent zeal for the glory of God, 
be not insensible to the unboundece 
confidence we repose in thy interces- 
sion. We entreat of thee in particular, 
by that tender charity which on earth 
would have made thee sacrifice ten 
thousand lives to benefit one soul, 
to obtain for our prelates and pastors 
that ardent zeal for the salvation of 
others and union with God which are 
essential to their holy vocations, as 
they were to thine. 
Beg for them from the adorable 
heart of Jesus a constant increase of 


ee ae ae ne ee A ER EON WD ese DD 


divine love, great purity of intention, 
a continual sense of the presence of 
God, and such a faithful imitation of 
thy virtues as may at length rank 
them among those who owe their per- 
fection and salvation to thy prayers 
and example. 

Penetrated with gratitude to God 
for their having been consecrated to 
His divine service, we now beseech 
thee to present their hearts to Jesus, 
our dear Saviour, that by thy inter- 
cession they may be replenished with 
the true spirit of their holy state. 

Obtain for our prelates and pastors, 
O glorious St. Paul, the particular 
favors we ask for them in this novena. 
[Here name your request.| We thank 
the Almighty for having crowned 
thy zeal with eternal glory and con- 
firmed thee forever in the possession 
of the divine and only object of thy 
affections. Amen.

PRAYER. 


O BLESSED Apostles St. Peter and 

St. Paul, I, [N.N.], take you 
this day for my special protectors 
and advocates with God. In all 
humility I rejoice with thee, blessed 
Peter, prince of the apostles, because 
thou art the rock whereon God has 
built His Church ; and I rejoice with 
thee too, blessed Paul, because thou 
wast chosen of God for a vessel ot 
election and preacher of the truth 
throughout the world. Ask for me, 
{ pray you both, a lively faith, firm 
hope, and perfect charity; entire 
detachment for myself, contempt of 
the world, patience in adversity, 
humility in prosperity, attention in 
prayer, purity of heart, right inten- 
tion in my works, diligence in the 
fulfilment of all the duties of my 
state cf life, constancy in my good 
resolutions, resignation to the holy 
will of God, perseverance in His

yrace even unto death, that by your 
joint intercession and your glorious 
wrerits I may overcome the tempta- 
‘tions of the world, of the flesh, and 
of the devil, and be made worthy to 
stand before the face of the chief and 
eternal Bishop of souls, Jesus Christ, 
Our Lord, to enjoy Him and to love 
- Him for all eternity, Who with the 
Father and Holy Ghost liveth and 
reigneth world without end. Amen. 

Our Father, Hail Mary, Glory be, 
etc.

Plenary indulgence on any of the feasts 
of St. Peter and St. Paul, or on any of 
the nine days preceding or within their 
octaves, to those who, being truly pen- 
itent, after confession and commun- 
ion, shall visit with devotion a church 
or an altar dedicated to these holy 
apostles and say there, with contrite 
heart, this prayer, praying for His Holi- 
ness.—Pius VI., July 28, 1778. 


Wovena of Wine Tuesdays in 
Donor of St. Antbony. 
(Feast, June 13th.) 
INSTRUCTION. 


“YT. ANTHONY’S death occurred on 
>) Friday, and his burial was ap. 


IN HONOR OF ST. ANTHONY. 34¢ 


poiated for the following Tuesday. No 
miracles had been performed through 
the saint’s intercession unti] the latter 
day, when the sacred remains were 
taken to their last resting-place. Tues- 
day was, therefore, from that time natu- 
sally chosen as a day especially appro- 
priate for devotion to St. Anthony, and 
that such choice was acceptable to God 
has been confirmed by innumerable indi- 
eationsever since. It was not, however. 
until nearly 4oo years after the death of. 
the saint that the peculiar novena of 
nine successive Tuesdays was recom- 
mended by himself. The occasion was 
as follows: In the year 1617, at Bo- 
logna, a lady of rank bescught St. 
Anthony with special fervor to obtain 
for her a particular grace. She was 
assured by an unwonted sense of in- 
terior consolation that her prayer had 
been heard, and in the following night 
she beheld the saint in a dream, en- 
circled with rays, and heard him say: 
“QO woman, for nine Tuesdays, one 
after the other, make visits to the 
church of my Order; on each of those 
days approach the holy sacraments of 
penance and of the altar; then pray 
before my picture, and what you ask 
you shall obtain.” The pious lady 
performed the novena according to 
these directions, and obtained what 
she had so fervently implored. 

The novena of nine Tuesdays in 
honor of St. Anthony of Padua rapidly 


342 NOVENA OF NINE TUESDAYS 


spread all over the Catholic world. 
How pleasing to God and St. Anthony 
the exercise of this devotion is we learn 
from the countless miracles and graces 
which God has been pleased to vouch- 
safe through its means. 


PREPARATORY PRAYER. 


O GLorious St. Anthony, safe ref- 
uge of the afflicted and dis- 
tressed, who through a miraculous 
revelation hast directed all those who 
seek thy aid to approach thy altar, 
with the promise that whoever visits 
it for nine consecutive Tuesdays and 
there piously invokes thee will feel 
the power of thy intercession, I, a 
poor sinner, encouraged by this 
promise, come to thee to-day with a 
firm hope, and I rejoice in the great 
honor which our holy mother the 
Church has shown to thee in placing 
Thy image on her altars. I pour 
forth my prayer to thee; I implore 
thy aid, thy protection, thy counsel, 
and thy blessing. Obtain for me, I 
beseech thee, my request in this 
necessity [zame if], provided it be 
aot opposed to the will of God and

the welfare of my soul. Should such, 
however. be the case, obtain for me 
such other graces as shall be con- 
ducive to my salvation. Through 
Christ, Our Lord. Amen.

PRAYER TO ST. ANTHONY FOR THE 
RESTORATION OF THINGS 
LOST OR STOLEN. 


BLESSED St. Anthony! the 

grace of God has made thee 
a powerful advocate in all necessities 
aud the patron for the cestoration 
of things lost or stolen: tc thee I turn 
to-day with childlike love and heart- 
felt confidence. Oh, how many thou- 
sands hast thou miraculously aided 
in the recovery of lost goods! Thou 
‘wast the counsellor of the erring, 
the comforter of the afflicted, the 
healer of the sick, the raiser of the 
dead, the deliverer of the captive, 
the refuge of the afflicted: to thee 
do I hasten. O blessed St. Anthony ! 
help me in my present affliction. I 
cecommend what I have lost to thy 


350 NOVENA OF NINE TUESDAYS 


care, in the secure hope that thou 
wilt restore it to me if it be to the 
greater glory of God and to the 
spiritual welfare of my soul, that I 
may praise and thank thee, in time 
and eternity, for thy glorious inter- 
cession in my behalf. Amen. 


YRAVER TO ST. ANTHONY. 


C1) salute thee, St. Anthony, lily 
of purity, ornament and glory 


of Christianity! We salute thee, 
great saint, cherub of wisdom and 
seraph of divine love! We rejoice 
at the favors Our Lord has so liberally 
bestowed on thee. In humility and 
confidence we entreat thee to help 
BS for we knaw that God has given 
thee charity and pity as well as 
power. Oh! then, behold our distress, 
our anxiety, our fears concerning— 
[here name your request]. We ask 
thee by the love thou dist feel towards 
the amiable little Jesus when He 
covered thee with His caresses. Oh! 
tell Him now of our wants. One 
sigh fromm that breast He so honored 
will crown our success, will fill us 
with joy. Oh! remember how com-

piete thy bliss was as thou didst hold 
Him to thy breast, didst press thy 
cheek to His, and didst listen to 
His angelic voice. 

Think of this and hear us for His 
condescending love.. If we could 
behold thee we would bathe thy feet 
with respectful tears and tell thee all 
we feel, all we fear for this 
But to see thee is not granted to us ; 
therefore we salute thee in spirit, O 
glorious favorite of God, and bow 
our guilty heads before thee in hum- 
ble reverence, while we raise our sad 
hearts full of hope towards heaven 
and thee, for He Who so often put 
Himself in thy arms will now fill 
thy hands with all we ask of thee. 
Give us, then, what we desire, angel 
of love, and we will publish thy 
grandeur, thereby to honor and 
glorify Him Who so biessed thee. 
Amen.

CLOSING PRAYER OF THE NOVENA, 


O BLESSED St. Anthony, my faith- 

ful intercessor, I have now, 
through the grace of God and thy 
assistance, finished the pious exer- 
cises in thy honor. I have, indeed, 
the will and desire to do all in my 
power for thy greater honor and 
glory, but thou knowest my weak- 
ness and misery and want of devotion, 
wherefore I beseech thee to make 
amends for all my failings, and to 
offer thy merits and good works for 
me, that I may find grace and hearing 
with God. Obtain for me through 
thy merits the blessing of a virtuous - 
life, a true knowledge of my God 
and of myself, a perfect mortification 
of my senses, anda complete triumph 
over all temptations. I place myself 
under thy special protection, assist 
me to follow thy example in life and 
come to my aid in death—in that 
dread moment let me experience the 
might of thy help. Amen. 


NOVENA TO ST. ALOYSIUS. 353 - 


Wovena to St. Alopsius, 


(Feast, June 21st. Novena begins June 
12th and ends June 2oth.) 


INSTRUCTION. 


6° name St. Aloysius Gonzaga is to 

call forth a picture of the mos: 
sublime and most amiable virtue. You 
cannot represent him without feeling 
the desire of sanctifying yourself and 
fulfilling as he did with faith, love, and 
perfection the ordinary duties of life. 
Prayer, study, pastime—all contribute 
equally alike in making his. day profit- 
able before the Lord; these days, so 
shortand few for him, have been worth 
an eternity of glory and happiness. 
His least actions have brought brilliant 
gems to his crown, which will encircle 
his brow forall eternity. Ifthe exam- 
ple of St. Aloysius induces us to work 
seriously at our salvation, his protec- 
tion assures us at the same time the 
necessary helps in the pursuit of it. 
Jesus and Mary can refuse nothing in 
heaven to those who have loved them 
Onearth. Renew, then, to-day your de- 
votion to this young and amiable saint; 
make the resolution to pray to him 
every day, to take him fora model and 
fora patron ; imitate his obedience and 
that angelic purity which makes him se 
dear to the spouse of pure souls and te 
tne Virgin of virgins 


354 NOVENA TO ST. ALOYSIUS. 


PRAYER TO ST. ALOYSIUS. 


O BLESSED Aloysius, adorned with 
angelic graces, I, thy most un- 
worthy suppliant, recommend special- 
ly to thee the chastity of my soul and 
body, praying thee by thy angelic 
purity to plead for me with Jesus 
Christ, the immaculate Lamb, and 
His most holy Mother, the Virgin of 
virgins, that they would vouchsafe to 
keep me from all grievous sin. Oh! 
never let me be defiled with any 
stain of impurity, but when thou 
dost see me in temptation, or in 
danger of falling, then remove far 
from my heart all bad thoughts and 
unclean desires, and awaken in me 
the memory of eternity to come and 
Jesus crucified ; impress deeply in 
my heart a sense of the holy fear of 
God; and thus, kindling in me the 
fire of divine love, enable me so to 
follow thy footsteps here on earth 
that, in heaven with thee, I may be 
made worthy to enjoy the vision of 
our God forever. Amen. 
Our Father, Hail Mary. 


Indulgence of 100 days once a day.— 
Pius VII., March 6, 1802.

Devotion for the Six Sundays in 
tbonor of St. Aloysius, 


3 order to excite among young 
Catholics devotion to St. Aloysius 
Gonzaga, Pope Clement XII. granted 
by two decrees, Dec. 11, 1738, and Jan. 
7, 1740, for all times, a plenary indul- 
gence on any one of the six Sundays 
preceding the feast of the saint, June 
2ist, or at any other time during the 
year, provided that these six Sun- 
days shall follow each other without 
interruption, and confession and com- 
munion be made, and prayers be said 
for the needs of the Church. On 
each of the six Sundays make pious 
Z meditations, vocal prayers, or some 
other work of piety in honor of St. 
Aioysius.. Though the Holy Father 
does not prescribe any particular prayer 
in honor of the saint, we are exhorted 
earnestly to meditate each Sunday on 
some of his virtues, and say six Our 
Fathers and six times the Glory be to 
the Father in memory of the six years 
during which he wasaReligious. These 
4rayers can be said at home, although 


360 NOVENA TO ST. STANISLAUS KOSTK4 


it were better to say them in a church 
where there is an altar of St. Aloysius. 


WAovena to St. Stanislaus RKostka. 


(Feast, Nov. 13th. Novena begins 
Nov. 4th and ends Nov. I2th.) 


PRAYER. 


O Gop, Who, among tlhe wonders 

of Thy grace, didst grant to St. 
Stanislaus from an early age the grace 
of an accomplished sanctity, grant us, 
we beseech Thee, the grace of re- 
deeming the time past by continually 
practising good works, that thus we 
may attain eternal rest. Grant us, 
also, O Lord, through the powerful 
intercession of this holy saint the 
favors we solicit through this novena. 
Amen.

Wovena to St. Tgnatius. 


(Feast, July 31st. Novena begins July 
22d and ends July 30th.) 

Before you begin the prayers of this 
novena, first make an act of the pres- 
ence of God, and disengage your mind: 
as far as possible from all worldly con- 
cerns and distractions; then, most pro- 
foundly humbling yourself, lift up your 
heart to God in adoration of the Most 
Blessed Trinity and, making the sign of 
the cross, say the following prayer: 


orD God of heaven and earth, 
Who art the beginning and 

end of all created things! behold. 
me in Thy divine presence, who am 
nothing but a wretched, miserable 


sinner; yet, O eternal Lord! I am 
24 


368 NOVENA TO ST. IGNATIUS. 


still the work of Thy sacred hands 
—not, indeed, made wretched by 
Thee, but brought to this wretched- 
ness and misery through the malice 
of the devil, the deceitful vanities of 
the world, and the allurements of the 
flesh. This deplorable state of sin has 
depiived me of those great favors 
Thy mercy had prepared for the 
support of those who should love 
and fear Thee. And just it is, O 
great God! that Thou shouldst with- 
hold from me whatever Thou didst 
intend for my support and comfort. 
This, O Lord! I acknowledge and 
confess. My infidelities and sins 
call forcibly on Thy divine majesty 
to arise and confound me. Against 
Thee, O Lord! I have sinned; my 
evils have been committed in Thy, 
sight; all the abominations of my 
_ lite are laid open to Thy all-seeing 
eye. 

But wretched and miserable as I 
am I still see Thy bleeding wounds, 
O dearest Redeemer! pleading for 
me, and turning from my guilty soul 
the wrath of Thy eternal Father ; 
wresting from His divine justice a 


NOVENA TO ST. IGNATIUS. 354, 


promise of pardon to all who will 
sincerely repent and return to their 
duty. Prostrated, therefore, at the 
foot of Thy cross, O bountiful Re- 
deemer! I offer to Thee a_ heart 
filled with sorrow and repentance for 
the many grievous sins of my past 
life. Oh! make this sorrow such as 
Thou wouldst have it. View the 
wounds of Thy blessed body, which 
let out the last drop of Thy sacred 
blood for me. Remember Thy love 
and Thy desire for my salvation, and 
grant the sincere petition of my 
heart. Let my heart love Thee, and 
detest all sin and infidelity for the 
love of ‘Thee. Let my heart be nailed 
in affection and love to Thy holy 
cross, and remain forever with Thee, 
that I may make some reparation for 
my many and grievous offences. 
Grant me this favor at present, O my 
God, and extend it to the hour of 
my death. Amen. 

O glorious St. Ignatius ! chosen in 
the designs of God from eternity to 
be the founder of His much-beloved 
society and the father of innumer- 
able saints, vouchsafe to pray for

me, and guide my petition in this 
novena, that I may ask nothing but 
what is to the greater honor and 
glory of Almighty God and the 
good of my soul; at least obtain for 
me, and for all now walking in igno- 
rance and the shades of death, light 
and salvation through the sacred 
wounds of Jesus Christ, Our Lord and 
Saviour. Amen. 

Here say three times Our Father, 
Hail Mary, and Glory beto the Father, 
etc., in honor of the Blessed Trinity.

Mm: holy and undivided Trinity !

the world by thy chosen servants, 
Blessed be the eternal Father, Wha 
has made choice of His faithful ser 
vant Ignatius as a fit instrument to 
propagate His glory and _ spread 
the knowledge of divine mysteries 
through the whole world. Blessed 
be God the Son, Who was pleased

Blessed be the Holy Ghost, Who did

Trinity ! be Thou adored and thanked 
by all Thy creatures, both in heaven

O blessed Ignatius ! by those great 
and singular favors which it has 
pleased God in His bounty to bestow 
on thee, by thy zeal and desire for 
the salvation of thy neighbor, permit 
thy humble client to beg thy power- 
ful assistance. Obtain of God's 
infinite goodness that my heart, in 
its affections and motions, may evur 


372 NOVENA TO ST. IGNATIUS. 


be guided by that sound doctrine 
which thou, by following the dictates 
of Heaven, hast preached to the 
world, and thereby brought salvation 
to thousands. It is to thee I owe in 
part the knowledge of God and my 
Christian duty. O most affectionate 
father ! permit not that the effect of 
thy prayers and toils should cease in 
our regard. Thou hast begun through 
the labors of thy children the work 
of our salvation; may I not confi- 
dently hope that whatever thou askest 
of God will be granted usr Behold! 
how, to the dishonor of God, religion 
is contemned, ignorance, wickedness, 
and sin prevail ; hell is crowded with 
innumerable souls; the blood of 
an all-bountiful God is lost—let all 
this, my loving protector, engage thy 
prayers in our behalf. Obtain for us 
that assistance from the Society of 
Jesus which our sins have hitherto 
deprived us of. I also beseech thee 
to obtain for me that particularly 
which I ask in this novena, if it be for 
the glory of God and the good of my 
soul. Amen.

y merciful God and loving Re-

Pious Lrercise of the Ten Sune 
days in Wonor of St. Tgnatius, 


HE Sovereign Pontiff, Clement 

XIII., by a decree of the S. Congr. 
of Indugences, Jan. 27, 1767, granted to 
all the faithful who, truly penitent, hav- 
ing confessed and communicated, upon 
the ten consecutive Sundays preceding 
the feast of St. Ignatius of Loyola, or 
uponany other tén consecutive Sundays 


376 NOVENA IN HONOR OF 


during the year, exercise themselves in 
pious meditation, or vocal prayer, or 
any other work of Christian piety to 
the glory of God and in honor of the 
same saint, and visit some church of 
the Society of Jesus, a plenary in- 
dulgence on each of the Sundays. In 
places where the said society does not 
exist the Sovereign Pontiff Gregory 
XVI., by a rescript of the same S. 
Congr. of Indulgences, Dec. 10, 1841, 
grauted that the faithful might gain 
these plenary indulgences by visiting 
their parish church and there praying 
for some space of time according to the 
intention of His Holiness.

His Holiness Pope Leo XIII., bya 
rescript of the S. Congr. of Indulgences, 
Nov. 21, 1885, granted to all the faith- 
ful who, upon the five Sundays which 
immediately precede the feast of the 
Sacred Stigmata of St. Francis of As- 
sisi, or upon any other five consecu- 
tive Sundays during the year, shall 
exercise themselves either in pious 
meditation, or in vocal prayer, or in 
any other work of Christian piety, in 
honor of the said sacred stigmata, a 
plenary indulgence once a year oneach 
of the five Sundays, provided that, be- 
ing truly penitent and having con- 
fessed and communicated, they visit 
some church or public oratory and 
there pray according to the intention 
of His Holiness. 


NOVENA TO ST. PAUL OF THE CROSS. 355 


Wovena in honor of St. Paul of 
the Cross, 


(Feast, April 28th. Novena begins 
April 19th and ends April 27th.) 


PRAYER. 


GLORIOUS St. Paul, thou who 

while on earth wast a mirror 
of innocence and a model of pen- 
ance! O hero of sanctity, specially 
chosen by God to meditate night and 
day on the most bitter passion of His 
only-begotten Son, and to propagate 
the same devotion throughout the 
world by word, by example, and 
by means of thy institute! O 
apostle, powerful in word and work, 
who didst spend thy life in bringing 
back the wandering souls of wretched 
sinners to the feet of the Crucified ! 
graciously look down from heaven 
upon my soul and hear my prayers. 
‘Obtain for me such a love towards 
Jesus suffering that, ever meditating 
on His passion, I may make His suf- 
ferings mine ; that I may recognize 
in the deep wounds of my Saviour 
the wickedness of my sins, and may 


386 NOVENA TO ST. PAUL OF THE CROSS. 


draw from them, as from so many 
founts of salvation, the grace of 
lamenting them bitterly, and a firm 
will to imitate thee in penance, if I 
have not followed thee in innocence. 
Procure for me, O St. Paul! the 
grace which here, at thy feet, I par- 
ticularly and earnestly beg of thee. 
[Here mention the grace desired. | 
Moreover, obtain for our holy 
mother the Church victory over 
her enemies; obtain conversion of 
sinners, and a return to the Catholic 
faith for all heretics, particularly for 
England, for which thou didst pray 
so much. Finally, intercede for 
me with God, that I may die a holy 
death, and come to enjoy Him with 
thee in heaven for all eternity, 
Amen. 

Our Father, Hai] Mary, Glory be 
to the Father. 

Indulgence of one year once a day. 
Plenary indulgence on the feast of the 
saint or on any day of the octave.— 
Pius IX., April 24, 1853; April 20, 1868. 

For making a novena in honor of St. 
Paul of the Cross an indulgence of 300 
days every day, and a plenary indul- 


gence at theclose of the novena.— Pius 
LA Octar 7s 1807.

388 NOVENA TO ST. MARY MAGDALEN, 


Wovena to St. Mary Magdalen. 


(Feast, July 22d. Novena begins July 
13th and ends July 2tst.) 


INSTRUCTION. 


HE Pharisee having prayed Jesusto 

sup with him, He entered into his 
house and seated Himself at the table. 
At the same time a woman of the city 
who led a bad life, knowing that Jesus 
was at table in the house of the Phar- 
isee, went there with an alabaster box 
ef perfume. She came to Jesus and 
commenced to wet His feet with het 
tears and to dry them with her hair, 
kissing them, and pouring the perfume 
over them. The Pharisee who had in- 
vited the Lord seeing this, said to him. 
self: ‘‘If that man were a prophet, He 
would surely know who touched Him, 
that she isa woman of bad reputation." 
Then, taking up her cause, the Lord 
said to Simon, the Pharisee: ‘‘ I have 
somewhat to say to thee, Simon.” 
Simon said: ‘‘ Master, speak;” and the 
Lord said: ‘‘ Simon, a creditor had two 
debtors. One owed him five hundred 
talents, and the other fifty, but as these 
debtors could not pay him, he forgave 
them both the debt. Which of these 
two loved the master more?’’ And 
Simon answered: ‘‘He to whom more 
had been forgiven.” And Jesus said 
“You have judged rightly;’’ and turn 


NOVENA YO ST. MARY MAGDALEN. 389 


ing towards the woman He said to 
Simon: ‘‘ Do you see that woman? I 
entered your house and you did not 
give Me water to wash My feet, but she 
has washed My feet with her tears and 
dried them with her hair; you did not 
give Me the kiss of welcome, while she 
has not ceased to kiss My feet ; you did 
not anoint My head with oil, and she 
has poured the perfume over My feet. 
For this reason I declare to you that 
many sins have been forgiven her, be- 
cause she has loved much.” Then He 
said to the woman: ‘‘Many sins are 
forgiven thee. Goin peace.” Follow- 
ing the example of St. Mary Magdalen, 
prostrate yourself at the feet of Jesus, 
bitterly deplore the sins with which 
you have sullied your soul, and, to make 
up for the want of contrition and love, 
offer to this great saint the following 
invocations. 


INVOCATIONS. 


S* Mary MacpaLEN, who in 
pouring out the perfumes on 
the feet of Jesus poured into His 
divine heart the sacred perfume of 
lively contrition and ardent love, I 
unite with thee. 

St. Mary Magdalen, who hast 
found more consolation in thy tears 
and humiliations at the feet of Jesus

than thou ever didst experience be- 
fore in all the pleasures of life and 
in the vain praises of the children ot 
men, I unite with thee. 

St. Mary Magdalen, who in offer- 
ing to God the passions of a too- 
fickle heart hast purified these stains 
and made it equal almost to the 
heart of a seraph, I unite with thee. 

St. Mary Magdalen, who after 
having run in the way of iniquity 
hast sanctified all thy steps in follow- 
ing Jesus Christ, I unite with thee. 

St. Mary Magdalen, who didst ex- 
change thy brilliant ornaments for 
the habit of poverty and penance, 
and didst gain by it the heart of the 
King of kings, I unite with thee. 

St. Mary Magdalen, who after thy 
conversion didst not taste any other 
joy than to find thyself at the feet 
of Jesus to listen to His divine word, 
[ unite with thee. 

St. Mary Magdalen, whom love 
retained at the tomb of Jesus, and 
who by the ardor of thy searching 
for Him didst merit to see that God- 
Saviour among the first, and to an- 
nounce His glorious resurrection to

mony of the goodness of the heart 
of Jesus towards those who sincerely 
turn to Him, pray for us. Amen.

fovena to St. Stepben, 
(Feast, Dec. 26th. Novena begins 
Dec. 17th and ends Dec. 25th.) 
PRAYER. 
Appy St. Stephen! great was 
the honor granted to thee in 
being the first to shed thy blood in 
testimony of the faith of Christ. O 
holy martyr, first ofall martyrs, obtain 
for us a living faith, a faith that is 
not dead, but that lives by good 
works, that thus our hope of salvation 
may not prove vain. Beg also, 
great saint, by the blood thou didst 
shed so willingly, that we may ob- 
tain the favor which we so ardently 
desire, and which we solicit through 
thy intercession in this novena. 
Amen.

Devotion of the Ten Wednesdays 
in thonor of St. Teresa. 


MONG various practices of piety in 
honor of the seraphic St. Teresa 

of Jesus there is one most highiy 
recommended and duly approved. It 
consists in dedicating to the saint ten 
consecutive Wednesdays, in memory 
and thanksgiving of the ten special 
favors she received from Heaven. viz. 


IN HONOR OF ST. TERESA. 399 


_ 1. The apparitions of Our Lord in 
the various states of His sacred pas. 
sion in order to stimulate her zeal to 
patiently endure the trials of life. 

2. The marvellous vision of the four 
precious stones in the cross of her 
rosary-beads. 

3. Manifestation of the Blessed Trin- 
ity in the interior of her soul. 

4. Contemplation of the glorious 
humanity of Jesus Christ. 

5. The sweetness of the precious 
blood of Jesus, which she tasted in 
holy communion. 

6. The ineffable bounty of Mary, who 
condescended to cover the saint with 
her mantle. 

7. The apparition of the Blessed Vir- 
gin Mary and St. Joseph, who clothed 
her in a robe of brilliant whiteness. 

8. Transfixion of her heart by the 
seraph’s dart. 

g. The assistance she received from 
the holy apostles SS. Peter and Paul 
in her needs. 

ro. Her elevation to the rank of 
spouse of Jesus Christ. 

N. B.—To obtain the favors desired 
one should sanctify each Wednesday by 
holy communion, prayer, and acts of 
mortification, especially by meditating 
on the grace which we desire to receive 
from heaven through the intercession 
of the saint. These ten pious prac- 
tices will be found in the history of hez 
life 

<0 


400 IN HONOR OF ST. TERESA. 


PRAYER. 


SERAPHIC and admirable St. 
Teresa, blessed by the Lord 
with an abundance of celestial 
light, obtain, I beseech thee, through 
thy powerful intercession, the grace 
for me that I may turn away my love 
from earthly vanity and direct it 
wholly to the heart of thy divine 
Master, deriving from it strength and 
perseverance to imitate the virtues 
which thou didst practise during 
life. 
O blessed Teresa, faithful teacher 
of the art of loving God above all 
things, teach me humility, obedience, 
purity, and a fervent zeal for the 
glory of God and the salvation of 
souls. 

St. Teresa, gifted with ecstatic 
contemplation, pray for me, that I 
may regulate my life in conformity 
with the precepts of my holy religion, 
and after a happy death may be ad- 
mitted to behold Our Lord face to 
face and to enjoy eternal happiness. 
Amen.

PRAYER. 

GLorIOUS St. Charles, the 
father of the clergy and the 
perfect model of holy prelates! thou 
art that good pastor who, like thy 
divine Master, didst give up thy life 
for thy flock, iftnot by thy death, at 
least by the numerous sacrifices of 
thy painful mission. Thy sanctified 
life on earth wasa spur to the most 
fervent, thy exemplary penance was 
a reproach to the slothful, and thy 
indefatigable zeal was the support of 
the Church. O great prelate! since 
the glory of God and the salvation 
of souls are the only objects of solici: 
tude to the blessed in heaven, vouch. 
safe to intercede for me now, and to 
offer up for the intention of this no- 
vena those fervent prayers which 
were so successful while thou wast on 
earth. [Here specify your request. |

among all the saints of God, one in 
whose intercession I should mos*

Wovena to St. Rose of Lima. 


1s ees Saint Rose, you were 
truly a sweet flower blooming 
on a rugged soil; you were indeed a 
rose’ among thorns, bearing with meek- 
ness and patience the stings of envious 
tongues, and preserving perfect purity 
and modesty amid the alluring blandish- 
ments of a deceitful world. To’ the 
sufferings inflicted on you by others you 
added the voluntary tortures of fast- 
ing and watching, of the discipline, of 
the crown of thorns and of the hair 
shirt, to subdue the flesh and to make 
yourself like to your heavenly Spouse. 
By the merits which you have thus 
gained with your divine Bridegroom, 
obtain for me the grace to bear my 
afflictions with patience, to remain pure 
and modest, to be meek and humble, to 
be faithful to the inspirations of the 
Holy Spirit, and so to mortify my pas- 
sions that I may be ever more pleas- 
ing and acceptable in the sight of my 
dear Lord and Saviour, Jesus Christ,

Wovena to the Archangel Gabriel. 


O BLESSED archangel Gabriel, we be- 
seech thee, do thou intercede for 
us at the throne of divine mercy in our 
present necessities that, as thou didst 
announce to Mary the mystery of the 
‘incarnation, so through thy patronage 
and prayers we may obtain the fruits 
of Our Lord’s mission among men: that 
Jesus may be to us, indeed, a Saviour, 
that we may one day see Him face to 
face, and with all the angels and the 
saints sing His praise forever. Amen. 


Wovena to the Arcbangel Rapbael. 


Coo archangel, St. Raphael, 
great prince of the heavenly court, 
illustrious by thy gifts of wisdom and 
grace, guide of travelers by land and 
sea, consoler of the unfortunate and 
refuge of sinners, I entreat thee to help 
me in all my needs and in all the trials 
of this life, as thou didst once assist 
the young Tobias in his journeying. And 
since thou art the “physician of God,” 
I humbly pray thee to heal my soul of 
its many infirmities and my body of the 
ills that afflict it, if this favor is for my

MY Lord and God, Who hast in 

Thine admirable providence given 
saints to Thy Church, in order that they 
may be a constant example to us of 
every virtue on earth, and our power- 
ful advocates and protectors with Thee 
in heaven, graciously hear the prayers 
of Thy servant, St. Rita, whom Thou 
didst give to the world as a model in 
all the different states of life; and 
grant that what we cannot obtain by 
our own weakness may be vouchsafed 
to us by her powerful intercession. 
Amen.

Daily Devotions. 
Morning Prayers, 


OM": depends on the manner in 
which we begin the day. We de- 


sire to make this day a very profitable 
one if it be in our power. We must re- 
member the fact that a good intention 
is necessary for all good works, and 
hence we begin the day by forming our 
intention. The morning offering of the 
League of the Sacred Heart embodies 
all that is necessary to direct our will 
for the day, and can be appropriately 
used. 

Intention.—O Jesus, through the. im- 
maculate heart of Mary I offer Thee 
the prayers, works, and sufferings of 
this day for all the intentions of Thy 
divine Heart in union with the holy 
sacrifice of the Mass. 

The first thoughts of a Christian in 
the mornin’ are those first fruits of 


427 


423 MORNING PRAYERS. 


which Almighty God was always most 
jealous. Endeavor to contract the 
good habit of raising your heart to God 
immediately on awakening to implore 
His protection and grace, that the day 
which His infinite goodness adds to 
your life may not add to your sins, but 
increase your merits and tend to His . 
glory. It may be the last in your life, 
perhaps you may never have another 
day to glorify God, to expiate your sins, 
to gain heaven, or to devote to any of 
the important duties of a Christian. 
Repeat pious ejaculations while you 
dress, that they may animate you not to 
throw away a Single moment of the day 
you are beginning. Le vour first re- 
membrance, then, be of God, your first 
action the sign of the cross, your first 
words an offering of your whole being 
tn your Creator. Do not lie lazily in 
_ bed after having had sufficient rest; it 
is time to be up and at your duties, 


*k In the name of the Father, and of 
the Son, and of the Holy Ghost. Amen.

THE ANGELUS. 


HE church bell rings three times a 
day, morning, noon, and night, 

to remind us of the Angelus. Let us 
then recite this prayer, as an indulgence 
of one hundred days is granted for each 
time it is said, anda plenary indulgence 
once a month to those who recite it 


DAILY .._.V6_4 INS. 425 


daily at the sound of the bell. This 
prayer is to be said kneeling, except 
on Saturday evening and throughout 
Sunday, when it is said standing. 

In Catholic countries all work is 
stopped, conversation ceases, and the 
peasants, bowing their heads, say the 
Angelus when they hear its bell. It is 
a beautiful practice, and one most 
pleasing to our blessed Lord and His 
holy Mother. Good Catholics should 
not neglect it.

Prayers at Mass. 


o™ holiest of all acts of religion, 
the greatest of all prayers, is the 
Mass. In it Jesus Christ gives to God 
an infinite glory and offers Himself for 
the remission of oursins. If we assist at 
Mass and join our prayers with Christ, 
the divine Mediator, our prayers will 
receive a greater dignity and strength. 
Let us appreciate it as a favor most de- 
serving of our gratitude that we can 
assist at it daily. Let us give God 
thanks for it, and study to draw from it 
the fruits of sanctification it ought to 
produce. Let us always assist at holy 
Mass with the most sincere, the most 
«avely, and the most affectionate piety. 

‘“In the divine Sacrifice of the Mass 
is immolated, in an unbloody manner. 
the same Jesus Christ Who once offered 
Himself in a bloody manner on the 
cross. All those who assist at this 
sacrifice with a sincere heart, a firm

OFFERING OF THE HOLY MASS. 


quar Father! permit me to 
offer Thee the Heart of Jesus 
Christ, Thy well-beloved Son, as He 
offers Himseltin sacrifice. Deign to 
accept on my behalf all the senti- 
ments, all the movements, all the 
affections of this divine Heart ; they 
are mine, since it is for me that He 
offers Himself. Receive them as the 
only homage worthy of Thee, the 
only means [ have of rendering Thee 
infinite glory. Receive them in sat- 
isfaction for my sins, and through 
His merits grant to me, and to those 
for whom I pray, all the graces we 
need. 

O Heart of Jesus, help me to assist 
at this adorable sacrifice with un- 
divided attention, profound respect, 
lively faith, and ardent love. May 
my heart be the victim of Thy love, 
that I may be united to Thee and 
participate in ‘hy merits.

- 8RAYER AT THE BEGINNING OF. MASS. 
(By St. Augustine.) 

O Gop! Who, for the redemption 

of the world, hast deigned to 
become man, to be rejected by the 
Jews, betrayed by the traitorous kiss 
of Judas, lovded with chains, led 
to sacrifice like an innocent lamb, 
shamefully dragged before Annas, 
Caiphas, Pilate, and Herod, and ac- 
cused by false witnesses; Who hast 
been scourged and spit upon, crowned 
with thorns, buffeted, struck with 
a reed ; Who didst permit Thy ene- 
mies to veil Thy face, to strip Thee 
of Thy clothes, and to nail Thee to 
the cross; Who wert willing to be 
confounded with robbers, to have 
vinegar and gall for Thy drink, to 
be pierced by a lance—O my Sav- 
iour, by all these cruel torments, 
upon which, unworthy as I am, I 
now meditate, and by Thy holy cross 
and death, deign to save me from 
the pains of hell, and to grant me, 
in virtue of this sacrifice, the remis- 
sion of my sins, an increase of grace, 
and eternal happiness. Amen.

THE LAST GOSPEL. 
According to St. John. 


a the beginning was the Word, 
and the Word was with God, 
and the Word was God; the same 
was in the beginning with God. All 
things were made by Him, and with- 
out Him was made nothing that was 
made ; in Him was life, and the life 
was the light of men, and the light 
shineth in darkness, and the darkness 
did not comprehend it. ‘There was 
aman sent from God, whose name 
wes John. This man came for 4 
vitness to give testimony of the light, 
ihat all men might believe through 
aim. He was not the light, but 


PRAYERS AT MASS. 46, 


was to give testimony of the light. 
That was the true light which en-.. 
lighteneth every man that cometh 
into this world. He was in the 
world, and the world was made by 
Him, and the world knew Him not. 
He came unto His own, and His 
own received Him not. But as 
many as received Him, to them He 
gaye power to become the sons of 
God; to those that believe in His 
name, who are born not of blood, 
nor of the will of the flesh, nor of 
the will of man, but of God. And 
the Word was made flesh [here the 
people kneel down), and dwelt 
among us; and we saw His glory, 
the glory as it were of the only-begot 
ten of the Father, full of grace anc 
truth. Thanks be to God.

INSTRUCTION. 


7 HE Sacrament of Penance has been 
1) given to us by God’s mercy that 
after having committed a sin we may 
quickly correct ourselves and make 
atonement for it. Init we exercise seri- 
ous virtues; we begin to know and 
humble ourselves. To reseive the sacra- 
ment with most benefit toour souls we 
must be actuated by a great faith, firmly- 
resolving to gro’y better and to lead 
purer lives. We should say with the 
Psalmist, ‘‘ Wasa me yet more from 
my iniquities and cleanse me from my 
sins.”’ Cal’ ea the Holy Gnost to in- 
spire us with light to see our faults. 
Examine our conscience, be sorry for 
our sins, eesolve to correct them, atone 
for therm by performing the penancé 
given by the priest, and lastly add 
seme little mortification of our owr

choosing. Go to confession as soon as 
possible after committing a sin which 
our conscience tells us is mortal. The

about.

EXAMINATION OF CONSCIENCE, 


st Commandment. — Ignorance 
of the great truths, prayers, 
etc.; neglecting to say your morning 
ov night prayers; going to sermons 
or prayers in Protestant churches ; 
giving scandal by it, or joining with 
them in worship ; reading Protestant 
books ; wilfully doubting, denying, 
or disbelieving the Catholic faith or 
speaking against it; despairing of 
God’s help, or expecting it without 
doing what He commands you ; mur- 
muring against God or His provi- 
dence ; not helping the poor ; leading 
others into sin ; consulting fortune- 
ellers or those who use charms, 
spells, cards; reading bad books ; 
behaving ill in church or to any 
holy person or thing; neglecting 
your penance ; receiving any sacra- 
ment with bad dispositions. (How 
often each sin ?) 

Second Commandment,—Speaking 
ill of God or the saints or what is 
sacred ; cursing ; an oath in a lie, or 
to do -vhat is sinful ; a custom of 
swearing ; breaking a lawful oath.

Chird Commandment. — Working 
on Sundays or holydays without ne- 
cessity; losing Mass on Sundays or 
holydays by your own neglect, or 
playing or talking during Mass, 
stopping away from Sunday-school, 
catechism, etc. 

Fourth Commandment.—Not lov- 
ing or helping your parents; striking 
them or showing disrespect to them, 
especially in their presence; cursing 
them or calling them bad names; 
disobedience to them; if in any 
great thing, for example, by going 
into dangerous company. 

Fifth Commandment.—Evil wishes 
on yourself or another, especially if 
from your heart; quarrelling, hatred, 
keeping spite, revenge, fighting, do- 
ing harm ito the life or health of 
yourself or another.

Immodest thoughts (if wilful); im- 
modest desires, words, looks, ac- 
tions, alone or with others, with 
married persons or relations or with 
anything; going into bad company, 
to bad dancing-houses, etc.; keeping 
dangerous company with persons of

AN ACT OF CONTRITION. 


O Lorp Jesus Curist, Thou over: 

flowing fountain of all mercy, 
behold I, Thy wretched and unhappy 
creature, return once more to Thee, 
and accuse myself in the bitterness 
of mysoul of not having kept my 
good resolutions. I have fallen into 
my former sins; I have served Thee 
with extreme negligence, and have 
been lukewarm and careless in my 
devotions; I have entangled myself 
too much in vain and_ transitory 
things ; 1 have far too eagerly sought 
earthly and hurtful pleasures, and in 
mnumerable ways have many times of- 
fended Thee, the sweetest spouse of 
my soul. And although Thine es- 
sential blessedness, O my God, can 
neither be increased nor diminished, 
forasmuch as ‘Thou canst have no 
need of me or of my goods, never- 
theless my sinful and careless life 
does in some sort tend to the preju- 
cice of Thy honor and glory. Thou 
knowest, O my God, what sorrow I 


DEVOTIONS FOR CONFESSION. 475 


icel for all these my sins. Where- 
tore, O most tender Father, I con- 
ress to Thee with most bitter contri- 
tion of heart all those sins whereof 
Thou knowest me guilty, most hum- 
bly beseeching that Thou Thyself 
wouldst supply my insufficiency from 
che treasures of that infinite love the 
‘ulness of which rests on Him Who 
aow sitteth at Thy right hand. For 
it is through Him that I pour forth 
before Thee my lamentation over my 
exceeding misery for having so often 
offended Thy goodness in thought, 
word, and deed. I offer and set 
forth before Thee now in satisfaction 
for my negligences and for the blot- 
ting out of my iniquities that sur- 
yassing prayer of Thy most beloved 
Son in the Garden of Gethsemani 
when the extreme and mysterious 
anguish of His soul wrung from His 
sacred body that bloody sweat. 
Amen. 


A PETITION TO JESUS BY ST. 
GERTRUDE. 


O MOST compassionate Jesus, fulf 
of pity and of mercy, Whe 
3h 


480 DEVOTIONS FOR CONFESSION. 


despisest not the sighing of the 
wretched, alas! my whole life is 
perished and gone without fruit, nor 
have I ever done anything good in 
Thy sight. To Thee, therefore, I

ency. Do Thou speak for me, do 
Thou satisfy for me. Wash away all 
the defilement of my sinful eyes with 
the pure tears of Thy most glorious 
eyes. By the sweet compassion of 
Thy blessed ears take away all the 
iniquity of my sinful ears. By the 
pure intention of Thy most holy 
thoughts, and by the ardent love of 
Thy pierced heart, wash away all the 
guilt of my evil thoughts and of my 
wicked heart. By the thrilling power 
of the sweet words of Thy blessed 
mouth blot out all the offences of 
my polluted mouth. By the perfec- 
tion of Thy actions and the crucifix- 
ion of Thy hands wash away all the 
offences of my impious hands. By 
the painful weariness of Thy blessed 
feet, and by their cruel piercing with 
the nails, wash away all the defile- 
ment of my sinful feet. By the ma- 
jestic innocence of Thy life and by

Thy unblemished holiness wash away 
the foulness of my corrupt life.

extinguish all the sins of my heart 
and my soul in the abundant streams 
of Thy precious blood, that by Thy 
holy merits I may be thoroughly 
cleansed, and hencefcrward keep 
all Thy commandments blameless, 
Amen. 


AT CONFESSION. 


Approach the confessional with the 
same recollectedness, silence, and mod- 
esty which would fill your heart if Christ 
Our Lord were seated there in person 
ready to hear your confession, and not 
the priest, whois really His representa. 
tive. When you kneel down, say: 
‘ Bless me, father, for I have sinned ;” 
and then tell when you last approached 
the sacraments and begin to accuse 
yourself of all your sins with humility 
and simplicity. After this confession 
oay attention to and accept the sacra- 

‘ental penance which is enjoined ; 
listen with docility to the voice of the 
»riest, and consult him upon any diffi- 
culty which may embarrass your mina. 
The penance must be performed within 


482 DEVOTIONS FOR CONFESSION. 


the time fixed by the confessor, and 
should he not fix the time it ought to 
be performed within a short time, for 
if put off for a long time we are apt to 
forget it, or even neglect it altogether.

infinite goodness. ‘Thou mightest

speakable and infinite mercy ! How

Devotions for Communion, 
INSTRUCTION. 


dERE is no action in the religious 

life of a Christian that demands 
more respect, piety, fervor, in short all 
highest sentiments of which the soulis 
capable, than the participation in the 
holy mysteries of religion, the recep- 
tion of the sacrament by excellence 
Holy Eucharist. Holy communion or 
the reception of the Holy Eucharist is 
the greatest happiness, the greatest 
grace of God. When we think that a 
poor creature receives into his soul the 
body and blood, the soul and divinity, 
of a God, reason is at a standstill—faith 
alone can raise us to the comprehen- 
sion of sucha favor. After the Mass 
holy communion is the holiest, the 
most sublime, the most glorious action 
which man -can offer to God in this 
world. A good communion can obtain 


348 


DEVOTIONS FOR COMMUNION. 499 


for us more graces than any other act 
of religion. by-it we can gain more 
merit than by all other practices of 
piety together. One holy communion 
received with the right dispositions 
would make usa saint. In it we wil\ 
find the very fountain of life, the bread 
of angels, the food of the strong, the 
hope of the elect, the germ of immor- 
tality, the pledge of eternal happiness. 
Hence it follows that we ought to give 
communion our greatest attention, 
make the most careful preparation for 
its reception, and seek to possess our- 
selves of the necessary dispositions to 
receive it worthily. 

But what are those dispositions 
which we ought to have in our heart? 
St. Paul, speaking of the preparation, 
bids us ‘‘to prove ourselves.” The 
Council of Trent, speaking of this text 
and explaining its meaning, says, ‘‘ The 
proving of the soul consists in a very 
serious self-examination;’”’ and should 
we find that we are guilty of a serious 
offence, we should purify ourselves by 
the Sacrament of Penance before ap- 
proaching the sacred table. 

Besides this essential preparation, 
there are dispositions and sentiments 
which the dignity and sanctity of this 


490 DEVOTIONS FOR COMMUNION. 


august sacrament call for. There isa 
preparation of the spirit, of the heart 
and the body. 

Preparation of the spirit: A lively 
faith, a profound respect, and a sincere 
humility. 

Preparation of the heart: A great 
sanctity, a desire to receive, and an ar- 
dent love. 

Preparation of the body: Not only 

outward respect and the strictest absti- 
nence from food and drink, but also a 
nortification of the exterior senses so 
as to insure the greatest attention with 
tne least distraction. 
' In your communion you can think 
with love and gratitude on the sublime 
attributes of God Who deigns to unite 
Himselfto you. He is the Second Per- 
son of the Blessed Trinity, the Word of 
the Eternal Father, the Word made 
flesh, the only Son of the Father, ful! of 
grace andtruth. Heis the beloved Sor 
of God, in Whom He was well pleased 
Whom He gave us as a model, and tc 
Whom we should listen. Heisthe true 
Messias, Who is our Way and our Life. 
the King of kings, the Lord of lords. 
Whose reign shall have no end. He 
only can lead usto the Father, He with: 
out Whom we can do nothing. 


DEVOTIONS FOR COMMUNION. 50> 


Oh! if we had the lively faith to real. 
ize that we are about to receive the 
spotless Lamb, the Lamb of God, not a 
human being, but God Himself, our 
soul would be a sanctuary, yes, heaven 
itself. 

Think that you are going to entertain 
Him Who created heaven and earth, 
He Who isthe joy of the angels and 
Saints in heaven.

O Lorp Jesus Curist, King of 

everlasting glory! behold, | de-

J desire to receive Thee, because it is 
Thy desire and Thou hast so or- 
dained: blessed be Thy name forever. 
I desire to come to Thee, like Mag- 
dalen, that I may be delivered from 
all my evils and embrace Thee, my 
only good. I desire to come to 
Thee that I may be happily united 
to Thee, that I may henceforth abide 


Key DEVOTIONS FOR COMMUNION. 


in Thee and Thou in me, and that 
nothing in life or death TAS evel 
separate me from ‘Thee. 

I desire in these holy mysteries 
lo commemorate, as Thou hast com: 
manded, all Thy sufferings: Thy 
agony and bloody sweat ; Thy being 
betrayed and apprehended ; all the 
reproaches and calumnies, all the 
scoffs and affronts, all the blows and 
buffets, Thou hast endured for me; 
Thy being scourged, crowned with 
thorns, and loaded with a heavy cross 
for my sins and for those of the 
whole world; Thy crucifixion and 
death, together with Thy glorious 
resurrection and triumphant ascen- 
sion. I adore Thee and give Thee 
thanks for all that Thou hast done 
and suffered for us; and for giving 
us in this Blessed Sacrament, this 
pledge of our redemption, this Victim 
of our ransom, this body and blood 
which was offered for us. 


AN ACT OF FAITH. 


T most firmly believe that in this 
holy sacrament Thou art truly 
present, O my Lord and my Saviour! 


DEVOTIONS FOR COMMUNION. 503 


that here is Thy body and blood, 
Thy soul and Thy divinity. I believe 
that Thou, true God and true man, 
art really here with all the treasures 
of Thy loving Heart ; that here Thou 
givest Thyself to us to make us par- 
takers of the fruits of Thy Passion 
and as a pledge of eternal life. I 
believe that there is no greater happi- 
ness in life than to receive ‘hee 
worthily, nor a greater misfortune 
than to receive Thee unworthily. 
O Heart of Jesus, here truly present, 
grant that my faith may be lively and 
efficacious, so that my heart may be 
worthy to receive Thee. 


AN ACT OF CONTRITION. 


O Lorp, I detest with my whole 
heart all the sins by which I 
have ever offended Thy divine maj- 
esty and grieved the Heart which 
has loved me so much. Behold, O 
Lord, I lay all my iniquities at Thy 
feet to be cancelled by Thy precious 
blood. Hear me and pardon me by 
that infinite love by which Thou 
hast shed Thy blood for me. Oh, let 


504 DEVOTIONS FOR COMMUNION. 


not Thy blood be shed in vain. I 
detest my sins, because they have 
offended Thy infinite goodness. By 
Thy grace I will never commit sin 
again; I will grieve for my offences 
as long as I live. Forgive me, dear 
Lord, for Thy mercy’s sake; pardon 
me in the clemency of ‘Thy heart, and 
for the future be Thou my protector, 
that I may never more offend Thee. 


AN ACT OF LOVE. 


O Lorp Jesus, the God of my 

heart and the life of my soul, 
as the hart panteth after the fountains 
of water, so does my soul pant after 
Thee, the source of life and the ocean 
ofall good. Oh, happy moment when 
I shall be admitted to the embrace of 
the living God, for Whom my soul 
ianguishes with love ; happy moment 
when my heart shall repose in the 
Heart of my God! Come, O my 
Jesus, take full possession of my heart 
forever. I offer it to ‘Thee without 
reserve ; I consecrate it to Thee for 
time and eternity. I love Thee with 
my whole’ soul above all things, and 
i desire to love Thee more and more.

A PRAYER FOR GRACE. 


O My God, Thou knowest my 

poverty and misery and that 
of myself 1 can do nothing. Thou 
knowest how unworthy I am of this 
infinite favor, and Thou alone canst 
make me worthy. By Thy Heart, 
so full of sanctity, sanctify my heart 
and prepare it to receive Thee. 
Cleanse my soul from its stains; 
clothe it with the nuptial garment of 
purity and charity; adorn it with the 
virtues of Thy Heart and make ita 
fit abode for Thee—so that this 
lieavenly visit which Thou deignest 
for my salvation may not by my 
unworthiness be perverted to my own 
destruction.

AN INVITATION TO JESUS BY ST- 
GERTRUDE. 


O Jesus, surpassingly sweet, Who 
hast said with Thine own gra- 
cious lips that Thy delights are to be 
with the children of men, my soul 
longeth for Thee, my heart yearneth 
for Thee. Wherefore I invite Thee 
to come unto me with all that devo- 
tion and that love wherewith any 
loving soul hath ever invited Thee to 
itself. Come, then, O most beloved 
spouse of my soul, come, O most be- 
loved and only love of my soul, 
come into the poor and wretched 
hovel of my heart. Come, thou 
heavenly Physician, come and heal 
my stricken soul. 


DEVOTIONS FOR COMMUNION. 50, 


AT COMMUNION. 


‘HEN the moment comes to aps 
proach the railing, excite in 
your hzart a new act of contrition while 
the Confiteor is recited and the priest 
pronounces the absolution in order to 
merit more and more the remission of 
your sins and obtain a perfect purity to 
receive the spotless Lamb. At the 
Agnus Dei and Domine non sum pour 
forth your heart in sentiments of love, 
contrition, respect, self-immolation, 
and tenderness. Say interiorly: 


HENCE comes it that this Lamb 

of God, that this God of 

purity and majesty, can vouchsafe to 

lower Himself to me, so unworthy of 

His presence? Yes, Lord, I am not 

worthy that Thou shouldst come te 

me; say but the word and my soul 
shall be healed.’’ 


Communicate with these sentiments 
and the religious feeling which faith 
inspires, and after receiving retire 
with respectful modesty, and remain 
some time in sweet and holy silence, 
with eyes cast down to avoid dis- 
traction. 


#98 DEVOTIONS FOR COMMUNION. 


AFTER COMMUNION, 


: HE time immediately following the 

reception of holy communion is the 
most precious, for then Jesus Christ is 
corporally present in your breast. If 
you have realized what you have beer. 
doing, you ought by this time to be full 
of God’s presence, deeply impressed 
with a lively faith that the living God 
has taken possession of your soul. 
You have many petitions to make, you 
have much to regret in your past life, 
and you have to promise a great deal 
for the future. 

Remain in church at least ten mir 
utes after communion. Do not mani- 
fest your careless forgetfulness of God's 
goodness by your haste to go home,

lution I have formed of never 
more offending Thee. Hear the 
prayers I address Thee for the Catho- 
lic Church, our Holy Father the 
Pope, for my dear parents, friends, 
and enemies, and for the poor suffer- 
ing souls in purgatory. 


PRAYER OF ST. GERTRUDE. 


O GOOD Jesus, O sweet Jesus, O 

Jesus most lovely, O Jesus my 

only salvation and my consolation, 

whence is this to me that Thou 

shouldst come unto me, and not ab- 
33 


512 DEVOTIONS FOR COMMUNION. 


hor to enter into my most polluted 
heart, defiled as it is with such 
exceeding stains and sins? Blessed 
be Thy coming unto me, and blessed 
the sweetness of the love which has 
constrained Thee to visit me, Thy 
poor and worthless creature. I cast 
myself now in the very dust before 
Thy face with the most profound 
humility and due reverence, and | 
kiss Thy most sacred feet and adore 
Thee, and I most humbly beseech 
Thee that Thou wouldst forgive me 
for having received Thee, my Lord, 
with so great negligence, distraction, 
and lack of devotion. 

Pardon me, O most tender Jesus, 
and through Thy iife-giving death 
blot out my great iniquity,and vouch- 
safe Thyself to supply all my defects 
and satisfy for all my negligences. 
Wherefore, I beseech Thee, deign to 
receive my communion into Thy 
Sacred Heart, and offer it to God 
the Father with all the love with 
which Thou didst offer Thyself upon 
the cross. 

And now, O good Jesus, I offer 
and make over to Thee my most un 


* DEVOTIONS FOR COMMUNION. 513 


worthy heart, beseeching ‘Thee that 
Thou wouldst wash it in that most 
precious water which Thy divine love 
caused to gush forth from Thy sweet- 
est Heert, fairer and more fragrant 
than the rose, glowing as a furnace 
with the ardor of Thy love. and that 
Thou wouldst sprinkle and revive it 
with the sweetest wine of ‘Thy most 
sacred blood, pressed from Thy 
divine Heart, as from a cluster of 
ripe grapes in the wine-press of Tby 
cross. Amen. 


PRAYER OF ST. IGNATIUS. 


ouL of Christ, sanctify me. 
Body of Christ, save me. 
Blood of Christ, inebriate me. 
Water from the side of Christ 
wash me. 
Passion of Christ. strengthen me 
O good Jesus, hear me. 
Within Thy wounds hide me. 
Suffer me not to be separated from 
Thee. 
From the malicious enemy defend 
me. 
In the hour of my death call me,

And bid me come to Thee, 

That with Thy saints I may praise 
Thee 

For ever and ever. Amen. 


An indulgence of seven years after 
holy communion. 


TO JESUS CRUCIFIED, 


O Lorp Jesus! by that bitterness 

which Thou didst suffer for 
me on the cross, chiefly when Thy 
blessed soul was separated from Thy 
body, have mercy on my soul, now 
and at its departure from this world, 
that it may be admitted to life ever- 
lasting. Amen. 


Vespers for Sunday. 


Lwstructtoi. 


fvery good Christian considers it as 
a kind of obligation to assist regularly at 
Vespers every Sunday. The entire day 
of the Lord belongs to Him: in the morn- 
ing you go to Mass, in the afternoon to 
Vespers. Any prayers may be recited 
during the time of the singing of the 
psalms. Read the following prayers 
and responses or occupy yourself with 
some pious reflections.

LESSED be God. 
Blessed be His holy name. 

Blessed be Jesus Christ, true God 
-and true man. 

Blessed be the name of Jcsus. 

Blessed be His Most Sacred Heart. 

Blessed be Jesus in the Most Holy 
Sacrament of the Altar. 

Blessed be the great Mother of God, 
Mary most holy. 

Blessed be her holy and immaculate 
conception. 

Blessed be the name of Mary, Virgin 


ind Mother. 
Blessed be St. Joseph, her most chaste 


spouse. 
Blessel be God in His angels and in 


His saints. 
(Induigence of two years, when said publicly 


after Mass or Benediction—Leo XIII, Feb. 2, 
1897.)
